Method: `scripts/skeleton_census_dump.cpp` generates fresh candidates exactly as the search does (`generateRandomProgram` with the CLI default `GenConfig`, deduplicated by text); 50 000 per target size, sizes 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, seed 90000. `scripts/skeleton_census.py` classifies them. Clean shapes, holes unconstrained: C1 = a top-level while whose body is exactly {store `arr[idx(d)] = e`, step of the driver `d = g(d)`} (prefix_sums, adjacent_differences); C2 = body exactly {`a = f(a, ..)`, `d = g(d)`} followed by a store that uses `a` (factorial_trailing_zeros, digital_root, reverse_number, lcm's gcd half, josephus).
Result: C2 is 9.4% at size 8 and 10.7 to 13.3% at sizes 12 to 28. C1 is 0.012 to 0.016% (about 1 in 7 000). A looser count of "any while with an element store" is under 1%, and most of those are noise.
Reading: the scalar-recurrence skeleton is plentiful (the scalar-loop piece of section 100 emits it), the array-update loop almost never appears. Combined with section 155 (fresh candidates per run are about 800 per size times about 35 sizes = 28 000, not the 300 000 assumed there): C2 gives about 3 400 candidates per run, C1 about 4. Upper bound on the fraction of the fill space covered if each skeleton candidate were replaced by 100 enumerated fills (the 3-case prefilter): factorial_trailing_zeros 1.0, digital_root 0.41, reverse_number 0.016, lcm 0.019, josephus 0.005; prefix_sums 0.004 and adjacent_differences 0.002 (C1 too rare). Without the prefilter every C2 problem is below 0.02.
Verdict: the piece is not built. Only two of eight problems are in reach (factorial_trailing_zeros, digital_root, the latter at 41% upper bound), and both need a new staged-evaluation path in generation. The two array-update problems would need the C1 skeleton injected on purpose, which is a hand-written shape and goes against the generic-pieces rule. The scalar-recurrence problems that stay out (reverse, lcm, josephus) fail on fill space, not on the skeleton. The bound is optimistic: it counts every C2 candidate as the right one, while the hole distribution inside C2 is not uniform. Predictions were not registered before this run (a measurement, not a test); the number that decides, 12% versus 0.014%, is the opposite of the section 155 guess that the C2 side would also be rare.

### 157. Why sorting yields one or two algorithms: staged funnel over fresh candidates (measurement plus one directed step; the search is unchanged)
Question (user): of bubble, selection, insertion, merge, quick, timsort, what stops the search from reaching them, grammar or search, and what would change that. Not pre-registered: measurement.
Method: `scripts/sort_census.cpp` (fresh candidates as in section 156; keeps those that sort every array of length 2..5 over {0,1,2}), `scripts/sort_milestones.py` (cumulative structural milestones on the printed text), `scripts/sort_wrap_test.py` (directed step). Skeletons `selectionsort,heapsort,mergesort,binarysearch` disabled through `ALGOSEARCH_DISABLE_SKELETON`; `skeleton_census_dump` gained an optional 4th argument (numVars).
Results:
- Skeletons on, size 40, 4 variables: 29% of fresh candidates sort, all through the selection skeleton. Skeletons off, 4 or 6 variables, 2 million candidates (sizes 20 to 60): 0 sorters.
- Skeletons off, 8 variables: 0.08% sort (241 of 300 000 at size 30, 247 at size 50). All 151 distinct programs are one template: two nested counted loops, compare of two elements, swap of the same pair (4 comparison operators).
- Funnel, 8 variables, 100 000 candidates: swap inside a loop 11.6%; swap under an element comparison 1.3%; swap of the compared pair 1.1%; that inside two nested loops 0.6%; adjacent pair (i-1, i) 0.18%; adjacent pair AND two nested loops 0 (also 0 in 180 000 candidates with 4 variables).
- Directed step: of the 349 fresh programs holding the single adjacent compare-swap pass, none sorts; wrapping the whole program in `vK = 0; while (vK < v0) { ...; vK = vK + 1; }` makes 16 of 172 and 14 of 177 sort (about 8%), i.e. bubble sort.
Reading: the grammar can express bubble (n passes), insertion (inner counter starts at the outer one and descends, always-descend variant) and gnome sort; what is missing is a generator move that composes a block with a counted outer repeat, and one that nests the adjacent pass under a second loop. With them bubble is one step away, so it is a composition gap, not a budget gap. Merge needs the 100-node hand-written skeleton (aux array plus two calls; 118b, 118c: 0 without it). Quick sort needs partition plus two calls on the pivot position; the generic divide-and-conquer shell never produced a recursive reduction (119). Timsort composes insertion and merge, out of reach. On the search side: every correct sorter scores 1.0, so fitness is flat once one is found, and the archive gets one representative per behavior class; the families found by sections 92 and 93 are the cheapest templates.
Not tested here: insertion sort with a descending inner counter (needs the second nesting move, not just the wrap); quick sort. Any lever (outer-repeat move, adjacent-under-nested move) is a new line and is not built without the user's go-ahead.

### 158. Outer-repeat move (`--outer-repeat P`, opt-in) -- design and predictions registered before running
Lever 1 of the sorting thread (section 157; levers 2 and 3 follow, see the end). Move: with probability P a fresh program (`generateRandomProgram`) or a mutated one (`growProgram`) is wrapped whole in `vK = 0; while (vK < v0) { <program>; vK = vK + 1; }`, vK the lowest variable in 1..numVars-1 the program does not use; no-op if the program has no loop or no free variable; fresh programs are generated 8 nodes smaller to pay for the wrapper. P = 0 draws nothing (byte-identical). Generic: it names no algorithm.
Arms. A (fresh funnel): `sort_census` at sizes 30 and 50, 8 variables, 300 000 candidates per size, seed 13, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off, P = 0.2 against the P = 0 numbers of section 157 (241 and 247 sorters, 0 with an adjacent pair). B (search): `run_sorting_seeds.sh`, seeds 1-8, 400 s each, same skeleton ablation, `EXTRA="--outer-repeat 0.2"` against no extra flag; every archive triaged with `scripts/triage_sorting.py`; a "bubble-family" program is a generalizing sorter whose compare-swap acts on an adjacent pair (i-1, i) inside two nested loops.
Predictions:
1. A: at least 5 bubble-family sorters among the 600 000 fresh candidates (baseline 0).
2. A: the exchange-template sorters are diluted by about P only: at least 60% of the baseline count (488), i.e. >= 293.
3. B: bubble-family programs in at least 4 of 8 seeds with the move; at most 1 of 8 in the control (section 46 saw 1 in 200 short seeds).
4. B: the control still finds the exchange template in at least 6 of 8 seeds, and the move does not lower that below 5 of 8.
Falsified if 1 or 3 fails; 2 and 4 are the harmlessness guards. Promotion to default would additionally need the joint battery (not part of this registration).
Levers kept in view: 2 = adjacent pass nested under a second loop with the inner counter starting at the outer one (insertion sort); 3 = recursion-side move (partition plus two calls on the pivot, merge without a hand-written skeleton). Both return after this result.

### 158 (results). Fresh-candidate predictions hold; in the search the move yields bubble sort in 1 of 8 seeds (prediction 3 falsified)
Data: `data/outer_repeat_ab_20261104/` (not versioned; `run.sh`, `control/`, `move/`, every seed triaged with `triage_sorting.py`). Seeds 1-8, 400 s each, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off, 8 variables, section 93's profile; move arm adds `--outer-repeat 0.2`.
Arm A (fresh, 600 000 candidates, `sort_census`, P = 0.2, seed 13): 501 sorters (baseline 488), 24 distinct bubble-family programs (baseline 0). Predictions 1 and 2 hold.
Arm B, generalizing programs per seed (control / move): seed 1 150/115, 2 31/137, 3 19/149, 4 1/1, 5 3/151, 6 1/11, 7 1/8, 8 1/147. Every one in the control arm is the exchange template; exchange is found in 8 of 8 seeds in both arms (prediction 4 holds).
Bubble family: the first count (adjacent pair inside two nested loops) gave 2 of 8 seeds in the move arm and 0 in the control, but the 70 programs of seed 5 are an exchange sort followed by a dead adjacent fragment, a false positive of the classifier. Read by hand, only seed 6 has genuine bubble sort (`v1 = 0; while (v1 < v0) { ...; v2 = 1; while (v2 < v0) { if (arr[v2-1] > arr[v2]) swap(...); v2 = v2 + 1; } v1 = v1 + 1; }`, 3 variants of 49-53 nodes, N=3 level), with a dead statement from the mutation.
Prediction 3 (bubble in at least 4 of 8 seeds with the move, at most 1 of 8 in the control): control 0 of 8 holds; move 1 of 8 (not 2 of 8, once the false positive is removed) is below 4: **falsified**. Predictions 1, 2, 4 hold.
Reading: the move works as a generator step (24 bubbles in 600 000 fresh candidates, from 0) and produces a clean bubble sort once in 8 searches, but the search does not keep it: the exchange template solves the same levels with fewer nodes and the same score 1.0, so it takes the beam and the archive (the flat-fitness argument of section 157). The move is necessary, not sufficient. Not promoted: it stays opt-in, no battery run. Next is lever 2 (adjacent pass nested under a second loop for insertion sort), with the selection side in view: any lever that reaches a second family still loses to the cheaper exchange template unless the search keeps a different cost profile alive; section 98's profile novelty is the existing tool for that and should be combined with these moves.

### 159. Nested adjacent pass (`--adjacent-nested P`, opt-in), alone and with profile novelty -- design and predictions registered before running
Lever 2 of the sorting thread (section 157), taken after section 158 showed that a generator move alone is not kept (the exchange template takes the beam at equal score). Move: with probability P a combinator adjacent pass (needs two free loop slots) becomes a nested adjacent pass with the inner range set by the outer counter, either descending (`vj = start+1; while (vj < n) { vi = vj; while (vi > start) { pass(vi); vi = vi - 1; } vj = vj + 1; }`, insertion sort when the pass is a compare-swap) or ascending (`while (vi <= vj)`). The pass keeps every relation of the adjacent loop; nothing names a sort. P = 0 draws nothing. Smoke test (100 000 fresh candidates, P = 0.3, size 40): 2 programs are insertion sort.
Arms. A (fresh): `sort_census`, sizes 30 and 50, 8 variables, 300 000 each, seed 13, same skeleton ablation, `--adjacent-nested 0.3` (7th argument 0, 8th 0.3) against the section 157 baseline (251 + 248 sorters... baseline 241 + 247 = 488, 0 adjacent). B (search): `run_sorting_seeds.sh`, seeds 1-8, 400 s, same profile and ablation as section 158. Control = section 158's control arm (same seeds and flags, reused). Treatments: B1 `--adjacent-nested 0.3`; B2 `--profile-novelty 0.5` alone; B3 both. Triage with `triage_sorting.py`; "insertion-form" = a generalizing program whose only compare-swap pairs are adjacent and that has a loop of the form `while (v > 0)` (descending inner counter), checked by hand on the smallest representative per seed.
Predictions:
1. A: at least 6 insertion-form sorters among the 600 000 fresh candidates (baseline 0).
2. A: exchange-template sorters at least 60% of 488.
3. B1 (move alone): insertion-form in at most 2 of 8 seeds (the exchange template wins the beam at equal score, as in section 158).
4. B3 (move + novelty): insertion-form in at least 3 of 8 seeds. This is the test of the selection side: if it fails, novelty by cost profile does not rescue a second family and another selection device is needed.
5. B2 (novelty alone): insertion-form in 0 of 8 seeds (the move is needed).
6. Exchange template found in at least 5 of 8 seeds in every arm.
Falsified if 1 or 4 fails. Promotion to default would need the joint battery. Lever 3 (recursion side: merge and quick) follows this section.

### 159 (results). The nested adjacent pass reaches insertion sort in 7 of 8 searches; novelty adds nothing; prediction 3 and 6 falsified
Data: `data/adjacent_nested_ab_20261104/` (not versioned; `run.sh`, arms `b1_move`, `b3_move_novelty`, `b2_novelty`; control = `data/outer_repeat_ab_20261104/control`, same seeds, flags and ablation). `triage_sorting.py` got one fix: a program that passes the tests but aborts on the larger cost inputs (recursion depth) now returns `ABORT_COST` instead of killing the whole file (b3 seed 6).
Arm A (fresh, 600 000 candidates, P = 0.3, seed 13): 501 sorters (baseline 488), 21 distinct insertion-form programs (baseline 0). Predictions 1 and 2 hold. (The first line of the design text above has a garbled baseline; the baseline is 241 + 247 = 488 from section 157.)
Arm B, per seed (generalizing programs; insertion-form; exchange template):
- control (section 158): insertion 0 of 8 seeds, exchange 8 of 8.
- B1 move alone: insertion in **7 of 8** seeds, exchange in 4 of 8 (seed 6: 136 generalizing programs, 79 of them insertion).
- B3 move + `--profile-novelty 0.5`: insertion in 6 of 8, exchange in 8 of 8; each seed keeps only 1 to 3 generalizing programs (novelty compresses the archive).
- B2 novelty alone: insertion in 0 of 8, exchange in 8 of 8.
Smallest representative (43 nodes, seed 6 of B1) is the textbook insertion sort in its always-descend form: `v4 = 1; while (v4 < v0) { v6 = v4; while (v6 > 0) { if (arr[v6-1] > arr[v6]) swap(...); v6 = v6 - 1; } v4 = v4 + 1; }`.
Scorecard: 1 holds, 2 holds, **3 falsified** (predicted at most 2 of 8 with the move alone, observed 7), 4 holds (6 >= 3) but is not informative since the move alone does better, 5 holds (0 of 8), **6 falsified** in one arm (the move alone has the exchange template in only 4 of 8 seeds, below 5).
Reading: my section 158 explanation (the cheaper exchange template always takes the beam) does not carry over: here the move alone keeps a second family in nearly every seed and even displaces exchange in half of them. Differences between 158 and 159 that could matter and are not separated: the move acts inside a combinator block (statement-local, composes with the mutation that follows) instead of wrapping the whole program; the pass is correct from the first draw instead of needing a repair; the always-descend insertion costs the same steps as exchange (about 700 000 at n = 256). The 158 reading (flat fitness and a cheaper template win) is therefore weakened, not refuted: it was a hypothesis for why the bubble was not kept, and bubble was kept in 1 of 8 only. Open question for later: does the outer-repeat move inside the combinator block (instead of around the whole program) keep bubble the way this move keeps insertion?
Status: opt-in, not promoted (no battery). Net for the sorting thread: with two small generic moves the search now holds exchange, insertion (7 of 8 seeds) and bubble (1 of 8) without a sort skeleton; selection, heap and merge still come from skeletons. Lever 3 (recursion side) is next.

### 160. Lever 3, measurement first: staged funnel toward merge sort and quick sort over fresh candidates (nothing built, search unchanged)
Not pre-registered: measurement, like section 157. Method: `scripts/skeleton_census_dump.cpp` (now with optional 5th and 6th arguments: recursion and aux biases) at sizes 40, 80, 120, 60 000 candidates each, seed 31, 8 variables, recursion 0.1 and aux 0.05 (the sorting profile of `run_sorting_seeds.sh`), skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off; `scripts/sort_rec_milestones.py` (text-level stages; hand-written quick sort and merge sort texts reach their last stages, checked); `scripts/sort_census.cpp` (its AUX and REC arguments are now biases) for sorters, 600 000 candidates at sizes 60 and 100.
Funnel (fraction of fresh candidates, same at the three sizes):
- any `call`: 11.5 to 11.7%
- a call with two plain lo/hi-like arguments: 4.1 to 4.4%
- such a call under an `if` guard: 2.0 to 2.2%
- two sibling calls with plain arguments in one block: 0.10 to 0.115% (60, 67, 69 of 60 000)
- sibling calls sharing a split value (`call(a, m); call(m or m+1, b)`): 0.012 to 0.013% (8, 7, 7)
- split value computed as a midpoint (`/ 2`): 0
- partition leaf (`if (...) { swap(...); b = b + 1; }`, a boundary advancing under a swap): 0, 0, 1 of 60 000
- merge take step (`aux[k] = arr[i]; i = i + 1;`): 0 of 180 000; copy-back loop (`arr[x] = aux[x]`): 1 of 60 000 at each size
- merge-like (split + midpoint + aux write + copy-back): 0. The "quick-like" text matches (14) are coincidences of unrelated blocks; none holds a partition.
- sorters among 1 200 000 candidates with recursion on: 914 (0.076%), all the nested compare-swap template of section 157; the 6 that contain `call` or `aux` carry them as inert decoys.
Reading: unlike sections 157 and 159, where one wrapper over a block present at 0.2% closed the gap, here the recursion shell is rare (sibling split calls 1e-4) and the leaf blocks that make it a sort are rarer still or absent (partition at most 1.7e-5, merge take step 0). A sort needs both at once with the guard and the argument wiring, so the joint rate is below 1e-9 per fresh candidate, against about 28 000 fresh candidates per run. The old two-call shell of section 119 is no longer a flag and produced no reduction when it was. No single wrapper move can close this: the missing parts are the leaves.
Candidate levers (not built, need the user's go-ahead): (3a) a generic partition block (a conditional swap that advances a boundary, Lomuto), useful beyond sorting (filtering, stable partition); it is the leaf quick sort needs. (3b) a range-recursion shell with a data-dependent split, to be tried only after 3a shows the leaf is reachable, then measured with this same funnel. Merge sort would additionally need the take-from-either-side step and a copy-back, three parts, and stays the last one.

### 161. Partition block (`--partition P`, opt-in), lever 3a -- design and predictions registered before running
Why: section 160 found the partition leaf at <= 1.7e-5 of fresh candidates and no merge/quick sorter. Move: with probability P a loop-capable statement slot (also inside `if`/`while`) is a Lomuto partition block `p = pivot; b = start; i = start; while (i < end) { if (arr[i] CMP p) { swap(arr[i], arr[b]); b = b + 1; } i = i + 1; }` with an optional pivot-placement swap; range [0, n) or, with recursion on, [v1, v2); pivot = last cell, first cell, or the key arr[0]; CMP drawn from <, <=, >, >=; 6 variables; one loop slot. Generic (names no sort; it is also a filter/partition-around-a-key leaf). P = 0 draws nothing. Printed variants checked by eye as standard Lomuto. By construction the leaf now appears in about P of fresh candidates, so the leaf rate is not a prediction; what is tested is whether it composes into a recursive sorter without the missing shell (3b).
Arms. A (fresh): `sort_census`, sizes 60 and 100, 600 000 candidates each, seed 41, 8 variables, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off, `--partition 0.1` (9th argument). Reference: section 160 (914 sorters in 1.2M, none recursive). B (search): `run_sorting_seeds.sh`, seeds 1-8, 400 s, same profile and ablation as 158/159 plus `EXTRA="--partition 0.1"` (recursion 0.1 and aux 0.05 already in the profile), triaged; "effective recursive sorter" = generalizing program whose triage row has call = True and whose smallest representative is read by hand and found to sort through its recursive calls (not an inert decoy).
Predictions:
1. A: fewer than 5 effective recursive sorters among the 1.2M fresh candidates (the shell with split wiring is still missing; expected 0).
2. B: an effective recursive sorter in at most 1 of 8 seeds (expected 0).
3. B: the exchange template still found in at least 5 of 8 seeds (harmlessness).
This is a gate for lever 3b: if 1 and 2 hold (as expected), the shell is needed on top of the leaf and the result says so; if either fails, partition alone suffices and 3b is skipped. Merge sort stays last.

### 161 (results). The partition block alone produces no recursive sorter, as predicted; the shell is the missing part
Data: `data/partition_ab_20261104/move` (not versioned; seeds 1-8, 400 s, `--partition 0.1`, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off; triage re-run for seeds 1-5 without the time limit that had cut them).
- Arm A (fresh, 1.2M candidates, seed 41): 740 sorters (361 + 379; section 160: 914 without the block), 3 contain `call`, all inert decoys beside an exchange sort. Effective recursive sorters: 0. **Prediction 1 holds** (< 5).
- Arm B: generalizing programs per seed 151, 149, 151, 146, 151, 7, 1, 5; programs with an executed recursive call (`recursive_calls_rev256 > 0`): **0 in every seed**. Many generalizing programs contain `call` (34, 17, 17, 97, 19 in seeds 1-5) and the partition block rides along in some (0 to 16 per seed), but the calls never run on the sorting path (the smallest read by hand: `if (v7 < v3) { call(v6, v2); }` in front of an exchange sort). **Prediction 2 holds** (0 of 8). Every seed's generalizing programs are the exchange template (**prediction 3 holds**, 8 of 8); adjacent-pass programs appear in 1 seed (3 programs), the partition leaf is dead weight.
Reading: the leaf (partition) is now cheap to reach, but a sorter needs it inside a size guard with two calls on the two sides of the split, and the search does not assemble that around the leaf in 400 s. This is the gate for lever 3b, and it says go: the shell is needed on top of the leaf. Section 162 follows (pre-authorized by the user).

### 162. Range-recursion shell around the partition block (`--range-shell P`, opt-in), lever 3b -- design and predictions registered before running
Pre-authorized by the user once section 161 came back negative as predicted. Move: with probability P a top-level slot (recursion on, 6 variables, a free loop slot) is `if (v2 - v1 > 1) { <partition block on [v1, v2)>; call(v1, b or b-1); call(b or b+1, v2); }`: the split is the partition boundary b of section 161's block; pivot cell, comparator, optional pivot-placement swap and the two offsets are drawn independently at random, so only some draws are a correct quick sort. P = 0 draws nothing. Smoke test (100 000 fresh candidates, P = 0.05, size 60, seed 5): 95 sorters against 50 without the shell, 39 programs with `call`; one printed program is the textbook Lomuto quick sort (`swap(arr[v1], arr[v4-1]); call(v1, v4-1); call(v4, v2)`).
Honest scope: this is assembly of a known algorithm from a piece that already carries the shape (size guard, two calls, split from a partition), like sections 121-124 for binary search; it is not discovery of quick sort. What it tests is whether the thread (leaf from 161, shell from 162) turns recursion from absent into reachable and kept by the search.
Arms. A (fresh): `sort_census`, sizes 60 and 100, 600 000 each, seed 41, 8 variables, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, same ablation, `--range-shell 0.05` (10th argument). B (search): `run_sorting_seeds.sh`, seeds 1-8, 400 s, same profile and ablation, `EXTRA="--range-shell 0.05"`, triaged; "effective recursive sorter" = generalizing program with `recursive_calls_rev256 > 0` and core exponent < 1.8.
Predictions:
1. A: at least 300 effective recursive sorters among the 1.2M fresh candidates (smoke extrapolates about 500; section 161: 0).
2. B: an effective recursive sorter in at least 5 of 8 seeds (section 161: 0 of 8).
3. B: the best (smallest core exponent) effective recursive sorter per seed has exponent below 1.6 in at least 3 of 8 seeds (an n log n shape, not an n^2 decoy).
4. B: the exchange template is still found in at least 4 of 8 seeds (the shell may displace it, as the insertion move did in section 159).
Falsified if 1 or 2 fails. Merge sort (take-from-either-side step, copy-back, shell with a midpoint split) stays last; the joint battery for the opt-in moves of 158, 159, 161 and 162 comes before any promotion.

### 162 (results). The shell makes recursive sorters appear fresh but the search keeps none: predictions 1, 2 and 3 falsified
Data: `data/range_shell_ab_20261105/move` (not versioned; seeds 1-8, 400 s, `--range-shell 0.05`, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off; triage run for all 8 seeds without time limit). `sort_census` now also prints how many sorters contain `call`.
- Arm A (fresh, 1.2M candidates): 1083 sorters (553 + 530; 914 in section 160), **172 with `call`** (103 at size 60, 69 at size 100); 161 distinct programs carry the shell guard. **Prediction 1 (>= 300) falsified**: the 100 000-candidate smoke test (50 extra sorters) overestimated the rate; the 1.2M count gives 14 per 100 000 candidates.
- Arm B, per seed (generalizing programs; executed recursive call): seed 1 3 (0), 2 0, 3 4 (0), 4 0, 5 0, 6 3 (0), 7 2 (0), 8 145 (0). **Effective recursive sorters: 0 of 8 seeds. Predictions 2 and 3 falsified.** Prediction 4 holds (the exchange template in 5 of 8 seeds). Three seeds (2, 4, 5) have no generalizing program at all in 400 s.
- Only 20 archived programs in all 8 seeds carry the shell (smallest 53 nodes), all WRONG or aborted (timeout, recursion depth, out of bounds); the smallest generalizing program per seed has 39 to 43 nodes (the exchange template).
Reading (a hypothesis, untested): the search tries sizes in increasing order and a level is solved, and the beam moves on, as soon as the 38 to 43 node exchange template is drawn; the shell needs about 50 nodes, so by the time its size is reached the levels are solved by the cheaper family. This also fits the earlier pattern: insertion sort (43 nodes, close to exchange) was kept in 7 of 8 seeds in section 159, bubble sort (49 to 53 nodes) in 1 of 8 in section 158, and the shell (>= 53 nodes) in 0 of 8. The reachability of section 162 is real (172 fresh recursive sorters) but the search does not retain what is larger than the cheapest family. The control is a run where the cheap family is not available at the early sizes.
Net: with `--partition` and `--range-shell` merge and quick sort are no longer unreachable in fresh generation (quick sort: 172 in 1.2M), but not kept by the search. The size-order hypothesis is the next thing to test; merge sort (three parts) is not worth building before that. Opt-in, not promoted, no battery run.

### 163. Size-order control for the range shell (`--start-size N`, opt-in) -- design and predictions registered before running
Question: is the shell kept in no search because the cheaper exchange template (39-43 nodes) closes the levels before size ~53 is tried (hypothesis of 162), or because the shell has another defect (pruning, recursion-depth budget, fitness)? Control: same profile as 162 (seeds 1-8, 400 s, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off), but `--range-shell 0.5` (shell dominates fresh programs) and `--start-size 50` (sizes below 50 are never tried). New flag `--start-size N` sets the first program size tried; 0 = default, byte-identical. Honest caveat: at size >= 50 the exchange template can still appear padded with dead code, so the control removes the ordering advantage, not the template.
Predictions (registered before the run): (1) if the ordering hypothesis holds, an effective recursive sorter (generalizing, recursive_calls_rev256 > 0, core_exponent < 1.8) is archived in at least 4 of 8 seeds; (2) if it is at most 1 of 8, the hypothesis is falsified and the defect lies elsewhere (pruning or recursion budget); 2-3 of 8 is inconclusive; (3) the smallest generalizing program per seed has at least 50 nodes in at least 6 of 8 seeds (a trivial consequence of the start size, checks the flag).

### 163 (results) [SUPERSEDED by the 163b correction: the count below missed depth-capped quick sorts; 7 of 8, hypothesis supported]. The size-order hypothesis is falsified: with the cheap sizes skipped and the shell dominant, still no recursive sorter is kept
Data: `data/size_order_ab_20261105/move` (not versioned; seeds 1-8, 400 s, `--range-shell 0.5 --start-size 50`, same profile as 162; triage_sorting.py on all 8).
- Generalizing programs per seed: 133, 2, 8, 4, 136, 135, 127, 146. **Effective recursive sorters (executed recursive call, exponent < 1.8): 0 of 8 seeds. Prediction 2 holds, prediction 1 falsified.** Archived programs that contain `call(` and generalize: 26, 0, 0, 0, 79, 30, 94, 121 across the seeds, but in none of them does the call execute (recursive_calls_rev256 = 0): the calls are dead code carried by the survivors.
- Prediction 3 is not meaningful as worded: the archived representative is the simplified program, so the smallest generalizing program per seed is 38 to 41 nodes (the exchange template again) even though no size below 50 was tried; the flag works (the log shows level 1 solved at size 51), the archive shrinks dead code.
Reading: removing the size-order advantage does not let the shell be kept. Together with 162 (172 fresh recursive sorters per 1.2M, so the shell is reachable) the defect is in how the search retains it: the shell is the pattern that is incorrect until all three parts (guard, partition, two calls with the right bounds) are correct at once, so growth/mutation from a candidate that is almost a quick sort scores no better than the exchange template with dead calls, and flat fitness (every correct sorter 1.0, near-misses by inversion count) gives no gradient toward the recursive form. Candidate explanations still untested: (a) partial-credit fitness does not rank near-quick-sorts above junk; (b) recursion-depth and step-limit aborts (ABORT:depth 2 to 11 per seed) kill near-misses at the training level; (c) the exchange template, once padded, outcompetes the shell on the same behavior-class slot. The next cheap discriminator is to take the 172 fresh recursive sorters of 162 and inject them as seeds into the beam (no fresh draw needed) to see whether the search keeps them: if they are dropped, the cause is selection (fitness, archive, class slot); if kept, the cause is that mutation/growth cannot reach them.
Opt-in `--start-size N` (0 = default, byte-identical). Moves of 158, 159, 161, 162 remain opt-in with no joint battery.

### 163b. Seeded beam: does selection keep a known recursive sorter? (`--seed-programs FILE`, opt-in) -- design and predictions registered before running
Question left by 163: the shell is reachable (162) but never retained. Is the cause selection (a recursive sorter present in the pool is dropped) or reach (mutation/growth never gets there from the beam)? Arm: same profile as 162 (seeds 1-8, 400 s, `--range-shell 0.05`, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off) plus `--seed-programs`, a file with the 172 fresh recursive sorters of 162 (regenerated by `sort_census` sizes 60/100, 600 000 each, seed 41, 103 + 69 with `call`, identical counts to 162), added to the candidate pool at the FIRST size step only, parsed by the new `language/Parser` (round-trip test over 300 generated programs). No flag = byte-identical. Honest caveat: at the first size step the level is N=1, where every program solves, so retention then depends on ranking (score, fitness, smaller size first) and the archive's class slots, which is exactly the selection under test.
Predictions (registered before the run): (1) if selection is the cause, no effective recursive sorter (generalizing, executed recursive call, exponent < 1.8) is in the final archive in at least 6 of 8 seeds (injected programs are dropped); (2) if reach is the cause, at least 5 of 8 seeds hold one; 2-4 of 8 is mixed. Expected: dropped, because the injected programs are 53 to 100 nodes and rank behind the 39-43 node exchange template on the size tie-break.

### 163b (results) and correction of the 162/163 readings: the "effective recursive sorter" count excluded correct quick sorts that hit the 200-deep recursion cap
Data: `data/seeded_beam_ab_20261105/move` (not versioned; seeds 1-8, 400 s, `--range-shell 0.05 --seed-programs` with the 172 recursive sorters of 162), triaged; counts for all four recursion runs by the new `scripts/recursive_kept.py`.
**Measurement error found.** The criterion used in 161-163 ("status OK, executed recursive call, exponent < 1.8") misses a program that passes every held-out test and then aborts on the sorted/reversed n = 256 cost inputs because the textbook last-element-pivot quick sort recurses n deep there and the interpreter caps recursion at 200: triage_sorting reports it as `ABORT_COST:depth`, not OK. Example, 163 seed 7, 53 nodes, found at level 7 and archived: `if ((v2 - v1) > 1) { pivot = arr[v2-1]; Lomuto partition with <; swap(pivot into place); call(v1, v4); call(v4+1, v2); }` plus a dead `arr[0] = arr[0]`. That is quick sort. I had read it as a failure ("aborted"). The tests of 162/163 had therefore not seen it.
Corrected counts (seeds with at least one correct recursive sorter in the archive, either OK-recursive or ABORT_COST:depth containing `call(`):
- 161 (`--partition`, no shell): 0 of 8 (unchanged, there is no shell).
- 162 (`--range-shell 0.05`): **2 of 8** (seeds 5, 8; smallest 55 nodes), not 0.
- 163 (`--range-shell 0.5 --start-size 50`): **7 of 8** (only seed 3 has none), 1 to 14 programs per seed, smallest 51 to 55 nodes.
- 163b (162 profile + 172 injected): 5 of 8 (seeds 2, 3, 4, 5, 7; 44 in seed 4), no better than the unseeded control and below 163; the archive does not say which of these programs descend from the injected ones, and the unseeded control already reaches 7 of 8, so injection shows no gain.
Prediction scorecard, corrected. 163: prediction 1 (>= 4 of 8 with the size order removed) **now holds (7 of 8)**; prediction 2 (<= 1 of 8) falsified. The size-order hypothesis of 162 is therefore **supported** by the control, not falsified: with sizes below 50 skipped and the shell dominant, the search keeps a real quick sort in almost every seed. 162: its predictions 2 and 3 stay falsified under the corrected count (2 of 8 is below 5 of 8), but the statement "0 of 8 searches keep one" is wrong and is replaced by "2 of 8". 163b: prediction 1 (injected programs dropped, <= 2 of 8 hold one) falsified, prediction 2 (>= 5 of 8) holds numerically, but the unseeded control reaches 7 of 8, so this run says nothing about selection versus reach.
What this changes: lever 3 (partition leaf + range-recursion shell) does reach and keep quick sort when the cheap exchange family is not allowed to close the levels first. What remains: (a) by default the shell is outcompeted by the cheaper family (162: 2 of 8); (b) the depth cap makes the program look broken to the triage and may also hurt it in the search when a level contains a sorted input of n > 200 (levels top out at N = 3000 in this profile, so the cap acts); (c) a median-of-three or random pivot would avoid the O(n) depth, but the grammar has no such piece. The four opt-in moves (158, 159, 161, 162) and `--start-size` stay opt-in; the joint battery is still not run.

### 164. Does the 200-deep recursion cap hurt quick sort inside the search? (`--max-recursion-depth N`, opt-in) -- design and predictions registered before running
Motivation: 163b found that the textbook last-pivot quick sort is archived but aborts the cost step on sorted/reversed n = 256 (depth n > 200). If levels contain sorted inputs of size > 200 (this profile goes up to N = 3000), the same cap may already be killing such programs during scoring. New flag sets the search interpreters' cap (default 200 = byte-identical; `scripts/triage_sorting.py` reads `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH`, default 200). Arm: the 163 configuration (seeds 1-8, 400 s, `--range-shell 0.5 --start-size 50`, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off) with `--max-recursion-depth 1000`, triaged with `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH=1000`. Baseline: 163, 7 of 8 seeds with a correct recursive sorter, 46 such programs in total (8, 2, 0, 1, 8, 11, 14, 2), all filed as ABORT_COST.
Predictions (registered before the run): (1) seeds with a correct recursive sorter stay at >= 7 of 8 (a higher cap cannot make fewer programs correct); (2) the total number of such programs across the 8 seeds exceeds 46 (the cap was removing some); falsified if <= 46; (3) with the cap at 1000 these programs triage as OK in at least 6 seeds, with a core exponent below 1.6 (O(n log n) on random input). If 2 fails and 3 holds, the cap only affected the triage's cost inputs, not the search.

### 164 (results). Raising the cap to 1000 makes the quick sorts triage as OK (exponent 1.12-1.13); the search count rises from 46 to 76 but one run per arm cannot separate it from noise
Data: `data/recursion_cap_ab_20261105/move` (not versioned; 163 configuration, `--max-recursion-depth 1000`, triaged with `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH=1000`; `scripts/recursive_kept.py` plus a per-seed core-exponent listing).
- Seeds with a correct recursive sorter: **7 of 8** (seed 3 none, same as 163). **Prediction 1 holds.**
- Programs per seed: 16, 2, 0, 1, 15, 13, 25, 4 = **76** against 46 in 163. **Prediction 2 holds** (76 > 46).
- All of them are now status OK (none depth-capped); core exponent 1.12 to 1.13 in every seed that has one (seven seeds); smallest 49 to 55 nodes. **Prediction 3 holds.**
Reading: the cap only changed how the programs are judged, which is what the triage showed in 163b; whether it also changed what the search keeps is not established. The count went up (76 vs 46) but each arm is a single 400 s run per seed with wall-clock-dependent progress, I have no run-to-run spread for this profile, and the baseline count was taken under triage cap 200, so the 30-program difference is suggestive, not a measured effect. Quick sort with the exponent of a real O(n log n) sort is now confirmed in the archive for 7 of 8 seeds when the cheap family is excluded from the early sizes.

### 165. Joint battery of the opt-in sorting moves, two rounds -- design and predictions registered before running
Arms (section 93's profile, 8 variables, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, 400 s per seed, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off, `run_sorting_seeds.sh`): BASE = no move; ALL = `--outer-repeat 0.2 --adjacent-nested 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05 --max-recursion-depth 1000` (the biases used in 158, 159, 161, 162, 164; `--start-size` is a control, not a candidate, so it is not in ALL). Two rounds with independent seed sets: round 1 seeds 1-8, round 2 seeds 9-16; order BASE-1, ALL-1, BASE-2, ALL-2. Triage after all four runs (not during them, so timed runs do not compete for CPU), with `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH` matching each arm (200 / 1000). Families per seed by the new `scripts/sort_families.py` on programs that pass every held-out test (OK or ABORT_COST:depth): exchange, bubble, insertion, quick. Validation on old data: insertion 7 of 8 on 159's move arm and quick 7 of 8 on 163 as before; it counts bubble in 2 of 8 seeds on 158's move arm, where the hand reading found 1 (seed 5 is an exchange sort with a dead adjacent fragment), so bubble hits are read by hand at the end.
Predictions (registered before running; "seeds" = seeds with at least one program of the family, per round, of 8):
1. BASE keeps insertion, bubble and quick in at most 1 seed each, in both rounds.
2. ALL keeps insertion in at least 5 seeds in both rounds (159 alone: 7 of 8; the other moves may dilute it).
3. ALL keeps quick in at least 2 seeds in both rounds (162 alone: 2 of 8); falsified if 0 in either round.
4. Mean families per seed: ALL >= 1.5 and BASE <= 1.2 in both rounds.
5. Noise: for every family and arm, the two rounds differ by at most 2 seeds; a larger difference means the earlier single-round comparisons were not safe.
6. Exchange is found in at least 5 of 8 seeds in every arm and round (no regression of the base family).
Decision rule written before the run: a move set is a promotion candidate only if ALL beats BASE on families per seed in both rounds (1 and 4 hold in both); ALL cannot attribute the gain to a single move, so a leave-one-out follows before any default changes. Time is not a gate.

### 165 (results). ALL keeps three families per seed against one for BASE, in both rounds; all six predictions hold
Data: `data/joint_battery_sorting_20261105` (not versioned; `run.sh`; `base_r1`, `all_r1` seeds 1-8, `base_r2`, `all_r2` seeds 9-16; 400 s per seed; 32 triages, `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH` 200 for BASE and 1000 for ALL; `scripts/sort_families.py`, cross-checked with `scripts/recursive_kept.py`).
Incident: I started the triage while the last seed (all_r2 seed 16) was still running, which loaded the CPU for about 1 minute of its 400 s. I killed the triage processes, moved that run to `contaminated/` and ran seed 16 again alone with the same command; all other runs were clean.
Seeds with at least one program of the family (of 8):
| arm | exchange | bubble* | insertion | quick | mean families per seed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BASE round 1 | 8 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.00 |
| ALL round 1 | 7 | 3* | 8 | 4 | 2.75 |
| BASE round 2 | 8 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1.00 |
| ALL round 2 | 8 | 2* | 8 | 4 | 2.75 |
\* Bubble by hand reading: the classifier's 5 hits are mostly insertion sorts with dead code (inner counter reset by an unrelated line, or a 358-node program carrying an insertion sort plus junk). The one genuine bubble sort is all_r2 seed 13 (45 nodes: `v1 = 0; while (v1 < v0) { v3 = 1; while (v3 < v0) { if (arr[v3-1] > arr[v3]) swap(...); v3++ } v1++ }`). So bubble is 0 of 8 and 1 of 8; the 3 and 2 in the table are the classifier's count, not the real one.
Quick by executed recursive call (recursive_kept.py): seeds 2, 5, 6, 8 in round 1 and 9, 11, 12, 14 in round 2, the same seeds the classifier found (4 and 4); the classifier counts more programs per seed than execute a call (seed 2: 87 vs 1), so per-seed counts of `quick` in the output are inflated by dead-call variants; only the seed counts are used.
Predictions: (1) BASE keeps insertion, bubble and quick in at most 1 seed each in both rounds: **holds** (0 everywhere). (2) ALL keeps insertion in at least 5 seeds in both rounds: **holds** (8 and 8). (3) ALL keeps quick in at least 2 seeds in both rounds: **holds** (4 and 4). (4) Mean families per seed ALL >= 1.5 and BASE <= 1.2: **holds** in both rounds (2.75 and 1.00). (5) Rounds differ by at most 2 seeds per family and arm: **holds** (largest difference 1: exchange 7 vs 8 and classifier-bubble 3 vs 2); the two rounds agree closely, so the single-round results of 158-164 were not noise-prone at the level of seed counts. (6) Exchange in at least 5 of 8 seeds in every arm and round: **holds** (8, 7, 8, 8).
Reading: the four moves together move the search from a single family (exchange, in every seed) to exchange plus insertion in 16 of 16 seeds plus quick sort in 8 of 16, with the same seed-level counts in two independent seed sets. This meets the decision rule written before the run (ALL beats BASE in both rounds), so ALL is a promotion candidate. It does not say which move carries it: insertion is plausibly the adjacent-nested move alone (159: 7 of 8) and quick the partition + shell + cap (162-164), outer repeat only reaches bubble sort and that stayed rare. Before any default changes: a leave-one-out (ALL minus one move at a time) to attribute and to find moves that can be dropped. Not done yet; nothing promoted. Merge sort is still not built.

### 166. Leave-one-out of the four opt-in sorting moves -- design and predictions registered before running
Question left by 165: which move carries which family, and is any of them droppable? Arms: ALL minus one move, each with the 165 profile (8 variables, recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, 400 s per seed, skeletons selectionsort/heapsort/mergesort/binarysearch off, `--max-recursion-depth 1000` kept in all arms): `no_outer` (no `--outer-repeat`), `no_adj` (no `--adjacent-nested`), `no_part` (no `--partition`), `no_shell` (no `--range-shell`); rest as in ALL (`--outer-repeat 0.2 --adjacent-nested 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`). One round of 8 seeds (1-8) per arm, compared with `all_r1` (same seeds: exchange 7, bubble 0 genuine, insertion 8, quick 4, 2.75 families per seed); 165 showed two rounds agree within 1 seed, so one round per arm is the budget; an ambiguous arm gets round 2 (seeds 9-16, compared with `all_r2`). The driver runs the triage itself after every search arm has finished (no CPU competition with timed runs). Families by `scripts/sort_families.py` (bubble read by hand), quick cross-checked by `scripts/recursive_kept.py`. `--partition` and `--range-shell` are separate biases but the shell calls the same partition generator, so removing `--partition` alone leaves the shell's own partition in place.
Predictions (registered before running; seed counts of 8, all_r1 = 8 insertion / 4 quick):
1. `no_adj`: insertion in at most 2 seeds (only this move can build the nested adjacent pass; BASE had 0); mean families per seed <= 2.0.
2. `no_shell`: quick in at most 1 seed (161: the partition alone gave no recursive sorter).
3. `no_part`: quick still in at least 3 seeds (the shell carries its own partition, so `--partition` is redundant given `--range-shell`).
4. `no_outer`: insertion in at least 7 seeds and quick in at least 3 (outer repeat only reaches bubble sort, which stayed rare); mean families per seed >= 2.5, which would make it droppable.
5. Exchange in at least 5 seeds in every arm.
Decision rule written before the run: a move is NEEDED if removing it lowers the seed count of some family by at least 3 relative to `all_r1`; DROPPABLE if no family changes by more than 1 seed and the mean families per seed stays within 0.25 of 2.75; anything else is ambiguous and gets round 2. A droppable move is removed from the promotion candidate; nothing is promoted in this section.

### 166 (results, round 1). Outer repeat is droppable, adjacent-nested and range-shell are needed, partition is ambiguous; without the adjacent pass quick sort is kept in 8 of 8 seeds
Data: `data/leave_one_out_sorting_20261105` (not versioned; `run.sh`, `summary.txt`; four arms, seeds 1-8, 400 s, triage run after all searches). Reference `all_r1` (165): exchange 7, insertion 8, quick 4 (by executed call), mean 2.75 families per seed. Bubble is the classifier's count and not reliable (165: mostly insertion sorts with dead code); it is not used for any decision.
| arm | exchange | insertion | quick (executed call) | mean families* |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ALL (165, round 1) | 7 | 8 | 4 | 2.75 |
| no_outer | 8 | 8 | 4 | 2.75 |
| no_adj | 8 | **0** | **8** | 2.12 |
| no_part | 5 | 7 | **2** | 2.00 |
| no_shell | 8 | 8 | **0** | 2.38 |
\* includes the classifier's bubble count, so only differences between arms matter.
Predictions: 1 (`no_adj`: insertion <= 2 seeds and mean <= 2.0): the insertion half holds (0), the mean half fails (2.12), so **falsified as written**; the mean stays above 2.0 because quick sort rises to 8 of 8. 2 (`no_shell`: quick <= 1): **holds** (0). 3 (`no_part`: quick >= 3): **falsified** (2). 4 (`no_outer`: insertion >= 7, quick >= 3, mean >= 2.5): **holds** (8, 4, 2.75). 5 (exchange >= 5 everywhere): **holds** (minimum 5, in `no_part`).
Decision rule applied: `--outer-repeat` is **DROPPABLE** (no family moves by more than 1 seed, mean identical); `--adjacent-nested` is **NEEDED** (insertion 8 -> 0); `--range-shell` is **NEEDED** (quick 4 -> 0); `--partition` is **AMBIGUOUS** (quick 4 -> 2 is below the threshold of 3, but the mean fell by 0.75 and exchange to 5), so it gets round 2 (seeds 9-16 against `all_r2`: exchange 8, insertion 8, quick 4).
Notable: dropping the adjacent pass raises quick sort from 4 to 8 of 8 seeds and every seed keeps one. Insertion (43 nodes) and the quick-sort shell (53 or more) compete for the same levels, the same size-order effect as 163 and now seen between two kept families; with both moves on, each seed tends to keep whichever closes the levels first. This is a single round per arm; 165 showed rounds within 1 seed, so differences of 3 or more are treated as real and 2 as not.

### 166 (results, round 2 of `no_part`). Partition is not needed and its effect is not separable from noise: still ambiguous, leaning positive
Data: `data/leave_one_out_sorting_20261105/no_part_r2` and `summary_r2.txt` (seeds 9-16, 400 s, triage after the search). Reference `all_r2` (165): exchange 8, insertion 8, quick 4 (executed call).
`no_part` round 2: exchange 6, insertion 6, quick **2** by executed call (seeds 9 and 13; the classifier says 3 because seed 12 has 68 dead-call variants, none executing), mean families 1.88. Differences against `all_r2`: exchange -2, insertion -2, quick -2. Round 1 was exchange -2, insertion -1, quick -2. Across the 16 seeds: quick 8 -> 4, insertion 16 -> 13, exchange 15 -> 11.
By the rule written before the run the arm stays **AMBIGUOUS**: no family drops by 3 or more in either round (needed), and the mean falls by 0.75 to 0.9 (not droppable). The direction is the same in both rounds for all three families, which the 165 noise level (rounds within 1 seed) makes unlikely to be pure chance, but no single difference exceeds my own threshold, so I do not call it needed. `--partition` is kept in the candidate set (it is not harmful and its direction is consistently positive).
Net of section 166: the reduced promotion candidate is ALL minus `--outer-repeat` (adjacent-nested 0.3, partition 0.1, range-shell 0.05, recursion cap 1000). The `no_outer` arm shows the same families as ALL on seeds 1-8 (round 2 for that arm was not run). Still not promoted: promoting needs the real defaults question answered (these moves change the generator for every problem unless gated to sorting; sections 158-165 only tested sorting), and the cross-problem battery is not run.

### 167. Cross-problem joint battery of the sorting moves -- design and predictions registered before running
Why: sections 158-166 tested the moves on sorting only, but `--adjacent-nested` and `--partition` change the generator for every problem that uses the combinator or has at least 6 variables, so promoting them needs the same joint battery as earlier pieces (sections 131, 150). Arms: base = current default (all moves at 0, byte-identical to the previous code); PIECE = `--adjacent-nested 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`, the reduced candidate of 166 (`--outer-repeat` dropped). `--max-recursion-depth` is left at 200 and `--range-shell` is inert for every problem that does not set `--recursion-call-bias` (it needs recursion and 6 variables), which is the real effect of a default promotion; the shell reaches only a run that opts into recursion, like the sorting profile. Same 77 problems, wall limits and per-problem settings as the digit-pieces battery (`scripts/run_sorting_moves_joint_battery.sh`, copied from `run_digit_pieces_joint_battery.sh`), 10 matched seeds 60100-60109, `--threads 1`, 8 jobs, benchmark 1 seed. Note that `sorting` here is the small battery version (n = 5, 4 variables), not the 3000/8-variable profile of 158-166.
Predictions (registered before running): (1) no problem drops by 2 or more seeds out of 10, base to PIECE; (2) the aggregate solved count of PIECE is not lower than base; (3) `sorting` itself does not drop by 2 or more. Problems with 4 or 5 variables cannot draw `--partition` (needs 6), so they are only exposed to `--adjacent-nested`; I expect most of the 77 problems to be unchanged within a seed or two. Promotion rule, as in earlier batteries: (1) and (2) must hold. If they do, the moves become generator defaults at these biases and the sorting profile (recursion 0.1, aux 0.05, 8 variables, depth cap 1000) stays the opt-in way to reach the recursive families; if (1) fails the moves stay opt-in and the failing problems are read.

### 167 (results). Cross-problem battery: no problem drops by 2, but the aggregate is 757 against 759, so prediction 2 fails and the promotion rule is not met
Data: `data/sorting_moves_joint_battery_20261106` (not versioned; 77 problems x 2 arms x 10 matched seeds 60100-60109, all 1540 runs finished). Tally by the new `scripts/tally_battery.py` (a run is solved if the N = max-n block reports held-out k/k); applied to the digit-pieces battery of section 150b it gives 761 / 757 against the 760 / 756 reported there, with the same per-problem differences, so it is within 1 per arm of the method used before.
Solved runs: **base 759, PIECE 757**. Per problem only two differ: elements_twice 10 -> 9 and majority_value 3 -> 2; every other problem is equal; no problem gains.
Predictions: (1) no per-problem drop of 2 or more: **holds** (largest drop 1). (2) PIECE aggregate not lower than base: **falsified** (757 < 759, by 2 runs). (3) `sorting` (n = 5, 4 variables) does not drop: **holds** (equal).
By the promotion rule written before the run (1 and 2 must hold) the moves are **not promoted** and stay opt-in. Reading, without softening the rule: both losses are one run on problems with few or noisy solves (section 131 recorded majority_value moving 2 -> 5 between arms with no explanation, and I called it noise there), there is no gain anywhere to offset them, and 2 runs of 1540 are inside the spread such batteries show, but they are exactly the aggregate the rule forbids. The earlier precedent for an offending result is an isolation (section 150c): the two problems at 30 seeds, base against PIECE, to see whether the drop is real. That has not been run.
What the battery does establish: the moves are close to inert outside sorting (75 of 77 problems identical in solved count), so the risk of promoting them is small and concentrated in two problems, and the only evidence of a gain stays in the sorting profile of 165-166.

### 168. Isolation of elements_twice and majority_value: base against PIECE at 30 seeds (registered before running)

Question: are the two one-run drops of section 167 (elements_twice 10 -> 9, majority_value 3 -> 2) real? Design as section 150c: the two problems, base against the PIECE arm of section 167 (`--adjacent-nested 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`), 30 matched seeds 60100-60129 (the first 10 are the section 167 runs), 180 s, benchmark 1 seed; solved counted with `scripts/tally_battery.py`. Script `scripts/run_sorting_moves_isolation.sh`, data `data/sorting_moves_isolation_20261106` (not committed).

Predictions, written before the run: (1) the difference per problem is within 2 solved of 30 (drops are noise), probability about 70%; (2) elements_twice base is about 28-30 of 30; (3) majority_value stays near 10 +- 4 in both arms. Decision rule: if both problems lose 3 or more with PIECE, the drop is real and the culprit move is isolated next; otherwise the aggregate failure of section 167 is attributed to noise and gating the moves to the sorting profile is proposed to the user, not applied.

### 168 (results). elements_twice is noise, majority_value loses 7 of 30 with PIECE

Solved of 30 (seeds 60100-60129, 180 s; data `data/sorting_moves_isolation_20261106`, not committed): elements_twice base 28, PIECE 27; majority_value base 14, PIECE 7. Total 42 against 34.

Predictions: (1) within 2 per problem: holds for elements_twice (-1), **falsified** for majority_value (-7); (2) elements_twice base 28-30: held (28); (3) majority_value near 10 +- 4 in both arms: held in numbers (14 and 7, at the edges). The decision rule did not foresee one problem real and one noise: elements_twice is noise; majority_value is a real cost of about 7 of 30 (Fisher two-sided p = 0.10, not significant, but the sign matches section 167 and the drop is a halving). The culprit move on majority_value is not isolated; the next step would be one arm per move on that problem. The moves stay opt-in, and gating them to the sorting profile remains the proposal.

### 169. One arm per move on majority_value (registered before running)

Question: which of the three moves causes majority_value 14 -> 7 (section 168)? Arms AN (`--adjacent-nested 0.3`), PA (`--partition 0.1`), RS (`--range-shell 0.05`), each alone, 30 seeds 60100-60129, 180 s; base (14) and PIECE (7) are reused from section 168 (same seeds and binary). Script `scripts/run_sorting_moves_majority_arms.sh`, data `data/sorting_moves_majority_arms_20261106` (not committed).

Predictions, written before the run: (1) exactly one arm falls to 9 or fewer and the other two stay at 12 or more (single culprit), probability about 45%; (2) the culprit is AN, because it changes the most statements per program (about 50%); (3) no arm reaches 7 or fewer other than the culprit. Reading rule: an arm counts as costly only at 9 or fewer (a drop of 5 or more); with 30 seeds and p = 0.10 for 14 against 7, 10-11 cannot be told from noise, and I will say so rather than name a culprit.

### 169 (results). majority_value: AN 9, PA 8, RS 13 of 30 (base 14, PIECE 7)

Solved of 30 (data `data/sorting_moves_majority_arms_20261106`, not committed): adjacent-nested alone 9, partition alone 8, range-shell alone 13. Predictions: (1) one culprit with the others at 12 or more: **falsified**, two arms fall (AN 9, PA 8) and only RS stays; (2) culprit AN: partly (AN is at the threshold of 9, PA is lower); (3) no other arm at 7 or fewer: held (PA 8). Reading by the rule: AN and PA each cost 5-6 of 30 and RS costs about 1; the PIECE loss (7) is roughly the two combined, not a single move. Neither is separable from noise at 30 seeds alone, but both point the same way. Supports gating adjacent-nested and partition to the sorting profile; range-shell is harmless on this problem. Not yet applied.

### 170. Two new opt-in moves for the missing families: selection block and merge-sort shell (smoke only, not a result)

Gaps in the sorting families covered by discovery (exchange, insertion, quick): selection, merge, counting. New opt-in generator moves, both default 0 (byte-identical): `--selection-block P` (`i = start; while (i < end) { m = i; j = i + 1; while (j < end) { if (arr[j] CMP arr[m]) m = j; j++ } swap(arr[i], arr[m]); i++ }`, range [0,n) or [v1,v2); 6 vars, 2 loop slots) and `--merge-shell P` (`if (v2 - v1 > 1) { mid; call(v1, mid); call(mid, v2); copy [v1,v2) to aux; merge back with three nested ifs for the empty-side cases }`; needs recursion and 7 vars; the comparison is drawn among Le, Lt, Ge, Gt so about half the draws are wrong-order merges).

Counting sort is structurally unreachable here, not just unfound: `aux` has the length of the input and the instances include negative and wide-range values, so a value used as an index goes out of bounds. No move was built for it.

Checks. Test `selection_block_and_merge_shell_can_emit_correct_sorters`: of 400 fresh programs at size 35 with selection bias 0.5, 248 sort every array of length up to 4 over {-1,0,1}; of 400 at size 80 with merge-shell bias 0.5, 229 do, the smallest at 81 nodes. 270 tests pass. Smoke (sorting profile of section 93, skeletons off, 150 s, 2 seeds each): `--selection-block 0.3 --start-size 30` keeps a correct selection sort in both seeds (37 nodes, exponent 1.97); `--merge-shell 0.5 --start-size 60` keeps none (81 nodes is the floor, 60 never reaches it); `--merge-shell 0.5 --start-size 80` keeps a correct merge sort in seed 1 (97 nodes, exponent 1.20, uses call and aux) and an exchange sort in seed 2. `scripts/sort_families.py` now also classifies selection and merge (merge = the quick test plus `arr[vK] = aux[...]`). These are smoke runs of two seeds, not a measurement of how often the families are found.

### 171. Calibration battery for the large families run (registered before running)

Question: with the five discovery moves available (adjacent-nested/insertion, partition+range-shell/quick, selection block, merge shell, outer-repeat dropped by section 166), how many sorting families does each size-gated arm keep per seed, and does the union reach all of exchange, insertion, quick, selection, merge? Four arms (`scripts/run_sorting_families_battery.sh`), seeds 1-4, 300 s each, section 93 profile, skeletons off, classifier `scripts/sort_families.py` after `triage_sorting.py` with `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH=1000`: A0 (start 0, all moves moderate: adjacent-nested 0.3, partition 0.1, range-shell 0.05, selection 0.1, merge 0.05), A30 (start 30, selection 0.3, adjacent-nested 0.3, partition 0.1), A55 (start 55, range-shell 0.5, adjacent-nested 0.1, partition 0.1), A80 (start 80, merge-shell 0.5, range-shell 0.05). Data `data/sorting_families_battery_20261107` (not committed).

Predictions, written before the run: (1) union over arms and seeds contains selection, quick and merge: 70%; (2) A0 keeps fewer families per seed than the gated arms' own target family rate, i.e. the size-order effect of sections 163 holds (selection 37 nodes and exchange close the levels before 81-node merge): merge appears in A80 in at least 2 of 4 seeds and in A0 in 0 or 1 of 4; (3) A55 keeps quick in at least 3 of 4 seeds (section 163 had 7 of 8); (4) A30 keeps selection in at least 3 of 4 seeds; (5) mean families per seed: A0 about 2, gated arms about 1.5 each. Decision: the large run uses the arms that reach a family in at least 2 of 4 seeds; an arm that reaches nothing is dropped or retuned.

### 171 (results). Calibration battery: five families reached, merge in 1 of 4 seeds only

Seeds 1-4, 300 s, `data/sorting_families_battery_20261107` (not committed); `triage_sorting.py` with `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH=1000`, families by `scripts/sort_families.py`. Seeds with the family, of 4 (mean families per seed): A0 exchange 4, insertion 4, selection 4 (3.00); A30 exchange 3, insertion 4, selection 4 (2.75); A55 exchange 3, insertion 3, quick 3 (2.25); A80 exchange 4, quick 1, merge 1 (1.50). The union over arms and seeds is exchange, insertion, quick, selection, merge (5 families, bubble 0). Per-program counts (selection 133-144 per seed in A0) are inflated by variants and are not used.

Predictions: (1) union contains selection, quick and merge: held (merge comes from one program set in A80 seed 3). (2) merge in A80 in at least 2 of 4 seeds: **falsified** (1 of 4); A0 keeps merge in 0 or 1: held (0). (3) A55 quick in at least 3 of 4: held (3). (4) A30 selection in at least 3 of 4: held (4). (5) mean families per seed A0 about 2, gated about 1.5: **partly falsified**: A0 is 3.00, A30 2.75, A55 2.25, A80 1.50; the mixed arm is not worse than the gated arms, the size gate does not raise the count.

Reading. Selection (37 nodes) needs no gate: A0 reaches it in 4 of 4. Quick needs `--range-shell 0.5` and `--start-size 55` (A0 reached it in 0 of 4 with range-shell 0.05). Merge is the weak one: one of four seeds, and A80 mixes `--range-shell 0.05`, which also makes quick shells that compete for the same levels; the large run therefore uses a merge-only arm (`--merge-shell 0.8`, no other shell).

### 172. Large families run: design (assembled, not launched)

Script `scripts/run_sorting_families_big.sh OUT [wall]`: four arms run in sequence, 1200 s per seed, seeds 1001+ (disjoint from section 171), section 93 profile with skeletons off. Arms and seeds (env-overridable): M80 merge-only (`--merge-shell 0.8 --start-size 80`) 16 seeds, A55 quick (`--range-shell 0.5 --start-size 55`, adjacent-nested 0.1, partition 0.1) 10 seeds, A0 all moves moderate 8 seeds, A30 selection and insertion 4 seeds (A0 already reaches them, kept only as a control): 38 seeds, about 12.7 h at the default. Report: `scripts/sorting_families_report.sh OUT` (triage 8 in parallel, then the family tally per arm). Predictions to register at launch: union reaches exchange, insertion, quick, selection and merge (85%); merge in M80 in at least 4 of 16 seeds (55%); no sixth family beyond bubble-like variants (90%).

Launched at 2026-11-07 06:44 with the default arm sizes (38 seeds, 1200 s), data `data/sorting_families_big_20261107` (not committed). Predictions registered at launch: (1) union reaches exchange, insertion, quick, selection and merge: 85%; (2) merge in M80 in at least 4 of 16 seeds: 55%; (3) no sixth family beyond bubble-like variants: 90%.

Run condition note (2026-11-07), corrected: from about 14:00 the machine ran on an external UPS in power-saving mode (cpu 799 MHz, governor `powersave`) and could not be raised. Each seed ends at 1200 s of wall clock but evaluates fewer candidates. The driver log restarts seed numbers per arm: the 16 M80 (merge) seeds ran 06:44-12:04 at full clock and are clean; A55 seeds 1001-1005 are clean, 1006 (13:44-14:04) is partly affected, 1007 and 1008 are affected, and 1009 was killed at 14:50 (6 min in, discarded). A family found in an affected seed is a valid positive; a family missing from one is not evidence of absence.

Paused at 14:50 (drivers killed by pid). Remaining at full clock: A55 1006-1010 (rerun 1006-1008, run 1009-1010), A0 1001-1008, A30 1001-1004. Resume with `ALGOSEARCH_DISABLE_SKELETON=selectionsort,heapsort,mergesort,binarysearch EXTRA="<arm flags of scripts/run_sorting_families_big.sh>" scripts/run_sorting_seeds.sh data/sorting_families_big_20261107/ARM 1200 SEEDS...`; move the affected seed directories to `affected_lowclock/` first so the rerun does not overwrite them.

### 172 (results). Large families run: five families reached, merge sort in 15 of 16 merge-arm seeds

Data `data/sorting_families_big_20261107` (not committed): 38 seeds of 1200 s. The A55 seeds 1006-1009 hold the full-clock reruns; the low-clock originals are in `affected_lowclock/` and not counted. Triage with `TRIAGE_MAX_DEPTH=1000`, families by `scripts/sort_families.py` (programs that pass every held-out test, per seed). Seeds with the family: **M80** (16 seeds, `--merge-shell 0.8 --start-size 80`): merge 15, exchange 12, others 0 (1.69 families per seed). **A55** (10, `--range-shell 0.5 --start-size 55`): quick 10, insertion 10, exchange 9, bubble 2, merge 1 (3.20). **A0** (8, all moves moderate): insertion 8, selection 8, exchange 6, bubble 2, quick 1, merge 1 (3.25). **A30** (4): selection 4, insertion 3, exchange 3 (2.50). Union: exchange, insertion, quick, selection, merge. Bubble counts (2 seeds in A55, 2 in A0) come from the classifier that confuses insertion with dead code and are not claimed.

The merge hits are genuine merge sorts, not artefacts: spot check of M80 seeds 1002, 1008, 1016 and 1001: status OK, exponent 1.20, 510 recursive calls on a reversed array of 256 (= 2n - 2), smallest program 93-95 nodes (one mutated variant carries a dead `v2 = v7`, the rest is `mid = (v1 + v2) / 2; call; call; copy to aux; three-way merge`); seed 1001 keeps only a 273-node variant. M80 seed 1011 is the one seed without merge.

Predictions (section 172): (1) union reaches exchange, insertion, quick, selection, merge: **held**. (2) merge in at least 4 of 16 M80 seeds: **held, far above** (15; the calibration of section 171 had 1 of 4 with the shared arm, so the shell-only arm was the fix). (3) no sixth family beyond bubble-like variants: held. Cost of coverage: each family needs its own arm. Quick needs `--range-shell 0.5` (A0 reaches it in 1 of 8, A30 in 0 of 4), merge needs the merge-only arm (A0 1 of 8, A55 1 of 10); selection and insertion come from the generic mix (A0 8 of 8 each). The moves stay opt-in; the large-run script is the recipe. Not covered: counting sort (structurally unreachable, section 170), heap sort, shell sort, comb/odd-even variants, Hoare or median-pivot quick sort; the classifier cannot tell exchange sub-variants apart.

### 173. Transfer of the sorting moves to neighbouring problems (registered before running)

Motivation: after section 172 the question is whether the moves are templates that only reproduce sorting or generic pieces that transfer. Section 167 could not show a gain because 74 of 77 suite problems already solve 10 of 10 at the base. Probe (4 seeds, 100 s, problem-specific pieces off with `--combinator-bias 0`, `--num-vars 8 --recursion-call-bias 0.1 --second-array-bias 0.05`): median 4/4, second_smallest 4/4, rotated_min 4/4 (no headroom, other skeletons solve them), min_gap 0/4, max_gap_sorted 0/4, count_distinct 0/4, inversion_count 0/4, lis 0/4 (headroom). fresh_kth_smallest was dropped: its array carries k in cell 0, so an in-place sort of the whole array does not fit. Counting sort and `--adjacent-nested` are not used: adjacent-nested only rewrites the combinator adjacent pass, which is off here.

Design (`scripts/run_sorting_transfer_battery.sh`, `scripts/tally_arms.py`): problems min_gap, max_gap_sorted, count_distinct (type 1: sort, then an adjacent pass: the transfer targets), inversion_count (pair counting, merge-based variants possible) and lis (negative control, not a sort-then-pass), arms CTRL (flags above only), SEL (`--selection-block 0.1`), MRG (`--merge-shell 0.1`), PQ (`--partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`), 10 matched seeds 61000-61009, 180 s, N = 8, benchmark 1 seed. Data `data/sorting_transfer_20261110` (not committed). Solved = N = 8 block with held-out k/k.

Predictions, written before the run: (1) CTRL solves 0 or 1 of 10 on every problem: 85%. (2) At least one move lifts at least one of the three sort-then-pass problems by 3 or more solved over CTRL: 35%. (3) lis stays at 0 or 1 in all arms: 90%. (4) inversion_count is not lifted by SEL or PQ (they do not count pairs): 85%; MRG lifts it by 3 or more: 10%. Decision rule: transfer is supported if a move lifts at least 2 of the 3 sort-then-pass problems by 3 or more with lis flat; partial if exactly one; none (template coverage confirmed) otherwise. An arm lift of 1-2 is noise at 10 seeds and will be reported as such.

### 173 (results). Floor effect: 0 solved in every arm, the test cannot separate template from transfer

Data `data/sorting_transfer_20261110` (not committed; 200 runs, all valid; the 15 `exit=0` are `lis` runs that ended early without a solution). Solved of 10, `scripts/tally_arms.py`: min_gap, max_gap_sorted, count_distinct, inversion_count and lis are 0 in CTRL, SEL, MRG and PQ. Logs show the search stuck at level 2 (N = 2) with best score about 56/74 and best size about 64, for every arm; MRG and PQ reach N = 2 in 3-4 seeds, SEL in none.

Predictions: (1) CTRL 0 or 1 everywhere: held (0). (2) a move lifts a sort-then-pass problem by 3 or more: not observed (0 everywhere). (3) lis flat: held. (4) inversion_count not lifted: held. By the registered rule the outcome is "none", but it is **not evidence for the template reading**: with the base at 0 and no partial credit for sorting alone, a floor effect hides any lift. Follow-up probes, not registered runs: `--start-level 5` on min_gap (4 seeds, 100 s, CTRL, SEL and MRG): all stuck at level 5 with best score 35-40/53, so even skipping the small levels the sort-then-pass composition is not found in this budget. A hand-written selection sort plus adjacent-minimum pass, injected with `--seed-programs` (with `--start-size` 80 to 125), reached only 55/74 at level 2 with best size 88 (the seed's size), so either the hand-written seed has an error I did not locate or the level-2 scoring differs from what I assumed; this probe is **unresolved** and no claim about reachability is made from it.

Reading: what the test established is that the moves do not make sort-then-pass problems solvable in this budget, which is weak negative evidence about transfer (the sort block is available and still not composed), but a fair test needs (a) a verified seed that solves the target, to show the composition is reachable, then (b) a measure with partial credit or a seeded half (the sort block fixed, the pass searched).

### 173 (follow-up). The hand-written composition is correct and reachable; the seeded search did not retain it (cause not located)

Test `selection_sort_then_adjacent_min_pass_solves_min_gap` (`tests/seed_reachability_tests.cpp`): the 80-node program `if (v0 > 1) { selection sort on [v1, v2); v7 = arr[1] - arr[0]; v6 = 1; while (v6 < v0) { if (arr[v6] - arr[v6 - 1] < v7) v7 = ...; v6++ } arr[0] = v7 } else { arr[0] = 0 }` is correct on all 430 min_gap instances of levels 1-6 (0 wrong). So the sort-then-pass composition is inside the language with the existing selection block; the open question of 173 is about search, not reachability. The earlier doubt about a seed error is closed: the seed is right.

The seeded searches (`--seed-programs`, with `--start-size` 80 to 125, and with `--start-level 2`) all stayed at 44-61 of 62-74 and never showed the seed as the best program (best size 23 to 88, not 80). So the seed was not scored as the correct program inside the beam: either `--start-size` does not gate the size loop at level 2 the way it did in section 163 (the first progress line at level 2 shows size 1) and the 100 s budget ended before size 80, or the seed is dropped by a filter between the pool and the scoring. Not resolved. Consequence: the seeded half-test proposed after the first 173 result needs this debugging first (print the seed's score when it enters the pool).

### 173 (seed debugging). Cause found: the `--benchmark` path ignored `--seed-programs`, `--start-size` and `--max-recursion-depth`

Added `ALGOSEARCH_DEBUG_SEED` (prints each injected seed's score when it is scored). Without `--benchmark` the min_gap seed enters at level 2, size step 80 and scores 62/62 (program size 80), so the seed and the injection were right. The seeded probes of section 173 all passed `--benchmark`, whose config is built separately in `runBenchmarkMode` and did not copy `startSize`, `maxRecursionDepth` or the seed programs: those runs were unseeded, which explains scores of 44-61 and best sizes of 23-88. Fixed: the benchmark config now copies the three fields (helper `loadSeedPrograms`); with the fix the same command finds the seed (62/62) and the benchmark reports held-out 28/28 at size 80. Scope of the bug: only runs that combined `--benchmark` with these three flags. Sections 163, 163b and 164 ran the non-benchmark path (`run_sorting_seeds.sh`) and are not affected; the transfer battery (173) used none of the three flags, so its 0-everywhere result stands, as a floor effect.

### 174. Fair transfer test: the seeded half (registered before running)

Context: section 173 gave 0 solved everywhere (floor), and a seed with only the sort block scores 15/62 at level 2 (no partial credit for "sorted, not answered"), ties with trivial programs and leaves the beam at once. The test therefore seeds a program that already solves levels 1-2 and asks whether the search generalises it. Seeds (`scripts/seeds/`): `gap_sort_first_two.txt` (selection sort on [v1, v2), then `arr[0] = arr[1] - arr[0]`, n < 2 gives 0; 50 nodes) and `gap_nosort_first_two.txt` (`|arr[0] - arr[1]|` by an if/else, n < 2 gives 0; 28 nodes); `ALGOSEARCH_DEBUG_SEED` confirms both score 62/62 at level 2 on both min_gap and max_gap_sorted (they agree for n <= 2). To generalise, the sorted seed only needs its last statement turned into an adjacent min (or max) pass; the unsorted seed has no sorted array to scan, so it needs an all-pairs structure.

Design (`scripts/run_sorting_seeded_half_battery.sh`): problems min_gap and max_gap_sorted; arms CTRL2 (unseeded, `--start-level 2`), S0 (no-sort seed), S1 (sort seed); common flags as section 173 CTRL (`--num-vars 8 --recursion-call-bias 0.1 --second-array-bias 0.05 --combinator-bias 0`, no move flags); 10 matched seeds 63000-63009, 180 s, N = 8, benchmark 1 seed (the `--benchmark` path now honours `--seed-programs`, fixed in section 173). Data `data/sorting_seeded_half_20261110` (not committed). Solved = N = 8 block with held-out k/k, `scripts/tally_arms.py`.

Predictions, written before the run: (1) CTRL2 solves 0 or 1 of 10 on both problems: 85%. (2) S0 solves 2 or fewer of 10 on both problems: 75%. (3) S1 solves 3 or more of 10 on at least one problem: 40%. (4) S1 exceeds S0 by 3 or more on both problems: 25%. Decision rule: (4) holds -> a correct sort prefix makes the pass reachable and the missing piece is composition under flat fitness (next: a lock-prefix or partial-credit move); S1 at 2 or fewer on both -> even with a correct sorted prefix and level-2 credit the search cannot add the pass in 180 s, so the block is not the bottleneck and the pass pieces (small generic pieces) are the next lever. Differences of 1-2 are noise at 10 seeds.

### 174 (results). The seeded half does not generalise either: S1 0, S0 0, CTRL2 0 of 10 on both problems

Data `data/sorting_seeded_half_20261110` (not committed; 60 runs, all hit the 180 s cap, exit 124). Solved of 10 at N = 8 with held-out k/k: min_gap CTRL2 0, S0 0, S1 0; max_gap_sorted CTRL2 0, S0 0, S1 0. Level reached: both seeded arms reach N = 3 in 10 of 10 seeds on both problems (the seed carries levels 1-2); unseeded CTRL2 reaches N = 3 in 4 of 10 and stays at N = 2 in 6. S1 stalls at level 3 with best score about 66/92 and best size 49, i.e. it keeps the 50-node seed and does not extend the final statement into a pass that works for n = 3.

Predictions: (1) CTRL2 0 or 1: held. (2) S0 2 or fewer: held. (3) S1 3 or more on at least one problem: **falsified** (0). (4) S1 exceeds S0 by 3 or more on both: **falsified**. By the registered rule (S1 at 2 or fewer on both): even with a correct sorted prefix and full credit through level 2, the search does not add the adjacent-minimum pass within 180 s. Reading: the sort block (the thing sections 170-172 added) is not the bottleneck for sort-then-pass problems; the pass, under all-or-nothing scoring at level 3 (66 of 92 reached by many wrong variants), is. This also answers the transfer question in its negative form: the moves do not transfer to the neighbouring problems in this budget, and the reason is not their absence of the sort but the absence of cheap, reusable pieces for the second half. Open caveat: one budget (180 s, beam 500), one seed prefix; an unrolled n = 3 fix (`min` of two differences) is the kind of local edit the search would have to find first, and the registered runs do not say how far away it is.

### 175. Is the adjacent pass alone findable? (registered before running)

Context: sections 173-174 turned off the combinator pieces (`--combinator-bias 0`) and found no sort-then-pass solution, even with a correct sorted prefix. With the default pieces on, min_gap and max_gap_sorted solved 10 of 10 in section 167, so the gap is not in the language nor in the sort block: the open question is whether the pass is the hard half under generic search, and whether the pieces that solve it can be composed with a free prefix. This test isolates the pass: problems that are one adjacent pass with no sorting (`max_adjacent_gap` max |a[i]-a[i-1]|, `max_rise` max a[i]-a[i-1], `min_step` min a[i]-a[i-1], `total_variation` sum |a[i]-a[i-1]|, `count_descents` count of a[i-1] > a[i]).

Design (`scripts/run_sorting_pass_battery.sh`): arms GEN (`--combinator-bias 0`) and PIECE (default pieces), other flags as section 173 (`--num-vars 8 --recursion-call-bias 0.1 --second-array-bias 0.05`), 10 matched seeds 64000-64009, 180 s, N = 8, benchmark 1 seed. Data `data/sorting_pass_20261111` (not committed). Solved = N = 8 held-out k/k, `scripts/tally_arms.py`.

Predictions, written before the run: (1) PIECE solves 9 or more of 10 on every problem: 85%. (2) GEN solves 2 or fewer of 10 on max_adjacent_gap, min_step and total_variation (they need an absolute value, a signed extremum init, or an accumulator): 70%. (3) GEN solves 3 or more of 10 on at least one of max_rise and count_descents: 50%.
