Decision rule: if GEN is at 2 or fewer on all five, generic search cannot find even the pass alone, so the pass is the hard half and the sort-then-pass failures of 173-174 are explained by pass difficulty; the next lever is composing the existing pass pieces with a free prefix (a combinator block placed after a sort block). If GEN solves three or more problems at 6 or more of 10, the pass is findable alone and the failure of 174 is a composition failure in the search (prefix lock or partial credit). Differences of 1-2 are noise.

### 175 (results)

Bloco de bateria `data/sorting_pass_20261111` (10 sementes pareadas 64000–64009, 180 s, N=8, terminou 11:17).
Resolvidos (bloco N=8, held-out k/k):

| problema | GEN (`--combinator-bias 0`) | PIECE (padrão) |
|---|---|---|
| count_descents | 0/10 | 10/10 |
| max_adjacent_gap | 0/10 | 10/10 |
| max_rise | 0/10 | 10/10 |
| min_step | 0/10 | 10/10 |
| total_variation | 0/10 | 10/10 |

Previsões: (1) PIECE ≥9/10 em todos — acertou. (2) GEN ≤2 em max_adjacent_gap, min_step, total_variation — acertou. (3) GEN ≥3 em max_rise ou count_descents — **errou** (0 e 0; eram a metade "fácil" da passada e a busca genérica também não as acha).
Regra de decisão: GEN ≤2 em todos → a passada, mesmo isolada, está fora do alcance da busca genérica; as peças do combinador é que a resolvem. As falhas de 173/174 se explicam por a passada ser a metade difícil, não pelo bloco de ordenação.
Próxima alavanca (precisa de go-ahead): compor as peças de passada existentes com um prefixo livre (bloco do combinador depois de um bloco de ordenação).

### 176 (registered): free prefix + combinator block

Motivation (175): the pass is only reachable through the combinator pieces; a sort block is reachable with the sort moves; the root loop stops after a combinator block, so a program "sort, then pass piece" is never generated. New opt-in move `--prefix-combinator P` (default 0, byte-identical): with probability P a root program is a free prefix (generated with `combinatorBias=0`, so `--selection-block/--partition/--range-shell` build it; about half the target size) followed by one combinator block.
Confound: the default pieces solve min_gap/max_gap_sorted/count_distinct without sorting (nested/distinct pair pieces). Both arms therefore run `--no-combinator-nested --no-combinator-distinct`; CTRL must score ~0 or the test is void.
Arms: CTRL (no prefix) vs PRE (`--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`). Problems min_gap, max_gap_sorted, count_distinct; 10 matched seeds 65000–65009, 180 s, N=8, common flags as 173–175. Script `scripts/run_sorting_prefix_battery.sh`.
Predictions: CTRL ≤1/10 on all three (75%); PRE ≥3/10 on at least one problem (40%); PRE ≥6/10 on at least two (15%).
Decision: PRE ≥3 on some problem → composition by prefix works, next = tune/enlarge; PRE ≤1 everywhere → prefix lottery is not enough under flat scoring, next = partial credit/prefix lock; CTRL >2 → void, find another control.

### 176 (results)

`data/sorting_prefix_20261111`, resolvidos (bloco N=8, held-out k/k), 10 sementes pareadas 65000–65009:

| problema | CTRL (sem prefixo) | PRE (`--prefix-combinator 0.3` + sort moves) |
|---|---|---|
| min_gap | 1/10 | 10/10 |
| max_gap_sorted | 6/10 | 10/10 |
| count_distinct | 10/10 | 10/10 |

Controle: previsão "CTRL ≤1 em todos" **errou**. count_distinct (10/10) e max_gap_sorted (6/10) são resolvidos sem prefixo por outras peças (estado/frequência etc., mesmo com nested e distinct desligados), então só min_gap é um teste limpo (CTRL 1 → PRE 10; Fisher p<0,001). Em count_distinct não há folga (teto) e em max_gap_sorted a diferença 6→10 é sugestiva, mas o controle é ruidoso.
Previsões: PRE ≥3 em algum problema — acertou (min_gap 10); PRE ≥6 em dois — acertou (3 problemas com ≥10, mas só um é limpo).
Mecanismo (inspeção dos 10 programas finais de PRE em min_gap): 5 são selection sort + passada adjacente (27 linhas) e 5 são heap sort + passada adjacente (63 linhas). Os esqueletos (heapsort etc.) NÃO foram desligados nesta bateria (`ALGOSEARCH_DISABLE_SKELETON` não definido), então metade da composição usa a ordenação do esqueleto e metade o bloco `--selection-block`; não separei as duas contribuições.
Decisão pela regra: PRE ≥3 → a composição por prefixo livre funciona. É o resultado oposto de 173/174: o que faltava era gerar "ordenar, depois passada" como programa inteiro; o lote de mutação/crescimento não chegava lá sob score tudo-ou-nada.
Pendente: repetir com esqueletos de ordenação desligados e com um controle melhor para max_gap_sorted/count_distinct (desligar também as peças de estado/frequência), e testar problemas em que nenhuma peça resolve sem ordenar (ex.: second_smallest, median).

### 177 (registered): 176 with skeletons off and a cleaner control

Same as 176 but `ALGOSEARCH_DISABLE_SKELETON=selectionsort,heapsort,mergesort,binarysearch` (the prefix must come from the sort moves), both arms also `--no-combinator-state --no-combinator-frequency`. Problems min_gap, max_gap_sorted, second_smallest, median; seeds 66000–66009; 180 s; N=8; script `scripts/run_sorting_prefix2_battery.sh`. Arms CTRL (no prefix) / PRE (`--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`).
Predictions: CTRL ≤2/10 on min_gap and max_gap_sorted (65%); PRE ≥5/10 on min_gap (65%, selection-block carried half of 176 alone); PRE ≥3/10 on max_gap_sorted (50%); second_smallest/median: PRE ≤2 (60%, the pass after the sort is `arr[1]`/middle read, which combinator pieces do not produce).
Decision: PRE ≥5 on min_gap with CTRL ≤2 → the prefix move alone (without skeletons) composes; second_smallest/median ≥3 → composition is general beyond adjacent passes; CTRL >2 anywhere → that problem void.

### 177 (results)

`data/sorting_prefix2_20261111` (esqueletos de ordenação desligados, peças state/frequency/nested/distinct desligadas), resolvidos em 10 sementes pareadas 66000–66009:

| problema | CTRL | PRE |
|---|---|---|
| min_gap | 0/10 | 10/10 |
| max_gap_sorted | 1/10 | 10/10 |
| second_smallest | 10/10 | 10/10 |
| median | 10/10 | 10/10 |

Previsões: CTRL ≤2 em min_gap e max_gap_sorted — acertou (0 e 1); PRE ≥5 em min_gap — acertou (10); PRE ≥3 em max_gap_sorted — acertou (10); PRE ≤2 em second_smallest/median — **errou, mas o teste é nulo**: o controle já resolve 10/10 (provavelmente por outra peça/skeleton de seleção ainda ativo, não investigado), então não há como medir composição ali.
Mecanismo: os programas finais de PRE em min_gap têm 26–31 linhas, o tamanho de selection sort + passada adjacente; sem esqueleto de ordenação, o `--selection-block` sozinho compõe.
Decisão: PRE ≥5 com CTRL ≤2 → o movimento de prefixo livre compõe ordenação e passada sem esqueletos (min_gap 0→10, max_gap_sorted 1→10, Fisher p<0,001 em ambos). Generalidade além da passada adjacente não testada (second_smallest/median sem controle limpo).

### 178 (registered): prefix with merge and quick families, and cost of `--prefix-combinator` across the suite

Two parts, run in sequence (`scripts/run_178_chain.sh`).
**(a) Families.** `scripts/run_sorting_prefix3_battery.sh`, data `data/sorting_prefix3_20261111`. Same setup as 177 (sorting skeletons disabled; nested/distinct/state/frequency pieces off). Arms: C80 (`--start-size 80`), MRG (C80 + `--prefix-combinator 0.3 --merge-shell 0.8`), C55 (`--start-size 55`), QS (C55 + `--prefix-combinator 0.3 --partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5`); min_gap and max_gap_sorted; seeds 67000–67009; 180 s; N=8.
Predictions: controls C80/C55 ≤2/10 (70%); MRG ≥5/10 on at least one problem (45%); QS ≥5/10 on at least one problem (45%). Decision: ≥5 with control ≤2 → prefix composes with that family; ≤1 everywhere for a family → the family's block is too costly/rare under the prefix move (size ~81 for merge) and the lever is not general.
**(b) Cost.** `scripts/run_prefix_joint_battery.sh`, data `data/prefix_joint_battery_20261111`: the section 167 suite (70 problems, same list, seeds 60100–60109, 180–240 s) with arms base and PRE (`--prefix-combinator 0.3` alone, no sort moves). Both arms rerun with current code (the benchmark path changed in 173; base also replicates section 167's base as a check).
Predictions: no per-problem drop of 2 or more and aggregate PRE ≥ base − 2 (promotion rule of 167) — 35%; majority_value and the frequency-family problems are the likeliest to lose. Decision: rule met → propose default (not applied); not met → keep opt-in and name the problems that lose.

### 178a (results): inconclusive, budget too short

`data/sorting_prefix3_20261111`, resolvidos em 10 sementes (67000–67009):

| problema | C80 | MRG | C55 | QS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| min_gap | 0 | 0 | 2 | 0 |
| max_gap_sorted | 1 | 0 | 3 | 1 |

Controles ≤2: C80 sim, C55 não em max_gap_sorted (3/10 sem prefixo; ruído de controle). MRG 0/20 e QS 1/20: as previsões "≥5" falharam. Mas nenhum braço, controles inclusos, passou do nível 3 em 180 s com `--start-size 55/80` (o último `LEVEL` impresso é 3 nas 39 execuções de min_gap lidas): cada candidato de 55–80 nós é caro e o tempo acaba antes de o prefixo ser testado de verdade. Isto é limite de orçamento, **não** evidência contra o prefixo com merge/quick. A seção 177 (selection, start-size padrão) segue como único resultado positivo.
Repetir com parede de 900 s (e, se preciso, 3000 s) depois do 178b, para não disputar CPU com ele. Decisão da regra registrada: nada decidido para merge/quick.

### 178b (results): `--prefix-combinator 0.3` on the 70-problem suite — promotion rule not met

`data/prefix_joint_battery_20261111`, 10 matched seeds 60100–60109, tally with `scripts/tally_battery.py` (PRE logs read as the second arm). Total base 759 (replicates section 167's base 759 exactly, so the 173 benchmark-path fix did not change defaults) against PRE 755 (−4). Per-problem differences: elements_twice 10 → 8, majority_value 3 → 2, max_drawdown 10 → 9; all other 67 problems equal.
Rule of 167 (no per-problem drop ≥2, aggregate not worse than −2): **not met** (elements_twice −2, aggregate −4). Prediction (35% that it is met) held as the minority outcome. The cost is small and spread over three problems that already lose a run or two under other opt-in moves (elements_twice also fell 10 → 9 in 167), so it looks like the generic price of replacing 30% of root programs, not a specific harm; not separated here (would need 30 seeds on elements_twice).
Decision: `--prefix-combinator` stays opt-in. Gating it to the sorting profile (together with `--selection-block`) is the natural place for it; not applied. Open: 178a (merge/quick) needs a longer wall; `FINDINGS.md`/`INDEX.md` lines for 173–178 still pending.

### 178a' (registered): 178a repeated with a 900 s wall

Same script, arms, problems and seeds (67000–67009) as 178a, `WALL=900` (`WALL=900 scripts/run_sorting_prefix3_battery.sh data/sorting_prefix3_900_20261112`). Predictions, from the 178a decision rule: controls C80/C55 ≤2/10 on each problem (60%; more time may let the controls climb); MRG ≥5/10 on at least one problem (40%); QS ≥5/10 on at least one problem (40%). First check: do the arms now pass level 3? If the controls reach level 8 but MRG/QS do not, the prefix move is the limit; if all still stop at level 3, 900 s is still too short and the next wall is 3000 s.

### 178a' (results): 900 s changes nothing; the 178a diagnosis ("budget too short") was wrong

`data/sorting_prefix3_900_20261112` (finished 08:14), resolvidos em 10 sementes:

| problema | C80 | MRG | C55 | QS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| min_gap | 0 | 0 | 2 | 1 |
| max_gap_sorted | 1 | 0 | 3 | 1 |

C80, MRG e C55 reproduzem **exatamente** os números de 180 s; só QS mudou (1+1 contra 0+1). Quase nenhuma execução bateu a parede (exit=124 só em QS: 9 e 6 de 10; zero nos outros braços): a busca termina sozinha (ex.: `Search finished in 694s` com 0 soluções) repetindo "LEVEL 3" (último nível 3 em 57 das 60 execuções sem QS, 3–5 em QS). Ou seja, com `--start-size 55/80` o nível 3 esgota os tamanhos sem fechar, e mais tempo não ajuda. A afirmação da 178a de que o limite era orçamento está **errada**; corrigida aqui.
Previsões: controles ≤2 (acertou em C80; C55 2/3 em torno do limite); MRG ≥5 e QS ≥5 em algum problema: **erraram** (0 e 1). Pela regra da 178a: ≤1 em toda parte para a família → o prefixo com merge/quick sob `--start-size` não funciona neste desenho. Não é evidência contra as famílias em si (merge/quick resolveram ordenação nas seções 171–172 com outros flags), mas contra a combinação start-size grande + prefixo + peças de combinador: programas de 55–80 nós no nível 1–3 raramente acertam tudo, e sem programas pequenos o feixe não avança.
Próximo desenho possível (não executado): sem `--start-size`, com sementes escritas à mão da família (como a 174, mas com o prefixo livre), ou prefixo de família forçado só quando o tamanho alvo ≥ tamanho mínimo da família.

### 178c (registered): sorter library as fixed prefix for the merge and quick families (option 1)

Why: 178a/a' showed `--start-size 55/80` with a free recursive prefix never gets past level 3. Here the sorter is given and the combinator block stays free. New opt-in `--prefix-programs FILE --prefix-library-count K` (default 0, byte-identical): each size step adds K candidates "random library sorter + one combinator block". A recursive prefix re-enters the whole program on `call`, so the block is wrapped in `if ((v2 - v1) >= v0)` (true only in the top-level call; sub-calls have strictly shorter ranges). Library programs are sorters discovered in the section 172 run (smallest correct single-family program per family from `solutions/triage`: `scripts/seeds/prefix_selection.txt` 12 lines, `prefix_quick.txt` 15, `prefix_merge.txt` 33). Tests: quick/merge sorter + guarded adjacent min pass solves min_gap (0 wrong of 430), generator emits the guard; 274 tests pass.
Design (`scripts/run_sorting_prefix4_battery.sh`, data `data/sorting_prefix4_20261112`): arms CTRL (no library), LS, LQ, LM (K=300), default start size, same flags as 177 (skeletons off; nested/distinct/state/frequency pieces off); min_gap and max_gap_sorted; seeds 68000–68009; 300 s; N=8.
Predictions: CTRL ≤2/10 on each (75%); LS ≥8/10 on both (70%, positive control); LQ ≥5/10 on at least one problem (55%); LM ≥5/10 on at least one problem (45%).
Decision: LQ/LM ≥5 → the recursive families compose when the sorter is available, so what is missing is finding the sorter inside the prefix move (next lever: prefix search for recursive sorters); LQ/LM ≤1 while LS works → something specific to recursion (depth cap, step cost, guard) blocks composition; LS <5 → the library mechanism itself is broken, results void.

### 178c (results): with the sorter given, quick and merge compose as well as selection

`data/sorting_prefix4_20261112`, resolvidos em 10 sementes pareadas 68000–68009 (300 s, N=8):

| problema | CTRL | LS (selection) | LQ (quick) | LM (merge) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| min_gap | 2 | 10 | 10 | 10 |
| max_gap_sorted | 3 | 10 | 10 | 10 |

Previsões: LS ≥8 nos dois — acertou; LQ ≥5 — acertou (10 e 10); LM ≥5 — acertou (10 e 10); CTRL ≤2 — acertou em min_gap (2), errou por 1 em max_gap_sorted (3, ruído de controle, mesma ordem de 177/178a).
Mecanismo: os 10 programas finais de LQ têm 31 linhas e os de LM 49 (prefixo + passada adjacente guardada por `if ((v2 - v1) >= v0)`), idênticos em tamanho entre sementes.
Decisão pela regra: LQ/LM ≥5 → as famílias recursivas compõem com a passada quando o ordenador está disponível; o que falta é **achar** o ordenador dentro do movimento de prefixo (178a/a' mostram que isso não ocorre com `--start-size` grande). Ressalva: os ordenadores da biblioteca foram descobertos pela própria busca (seção 172), mas aqui são injetados prontos, então isto prova composição, não descoberta conjunta. Próximo passo possível: prefixo livre gerado por famílias (como a 177 com selection) mas para quick/merge sem `--start-size`, p.ex. sorteando o prefixo recursivo de uma biblioteca de fragmentos (partição, mesclagem) em vez do programa inteiro.

### 179 (registered): free recursive prefix with the top-level guard, default start size

Found while building 178c: `--prefix-combinator` (section 176) appended the combinator block **unguarded**, so with a recursive prefix (quick/merge shell) the block re-ran inside every `call` and corrupted the array. Hence the 178a/178a' arms MRG and QS (0/20 and 1/20) were affected by this bug in addition to the `--start-size` stall; their "no evidence against the family" conclusion stands only in the weaker sense that they did not test it. Section 176/177 (selection prefix, no `call`) are unaffected. Fix: the same `if ((v2 - v1) >= v0)` guard as 178c is now applied whenever the free prefix contains a Call (`appendCombinatorBlock`); non-recursive output is byte-identical. 274 tests pass.
Design (`scripts/run_sorting_prefix5_battery.sh`, data `data/sorting_prefix5_20261112`): default start size (no `--start-size`), skeletons off, nested/distinct/state/frequency pieces off; arms CTRL, QS2 (`--prefix-combinator 0.3 --partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5`), MRG2 (`--prefix-combinator 0.3 --merge-shell 0.8`); min_gap, max_gap_sorted; seeds 69000–69009; 600 s; N=8.
Predictions: CTRL ≤2/10 on each (70%); QS2 ≥3/10 on at least one problem (40%); MRG2 ≥3/10 on at least one problem (25%; the merge shell is rarer and larger).
Decision: ≥3 with CTRL ≤2 for a family → the free recursive prefix composes (the guard was the missing piece) and the gap between composition (178c) and discovery closes for that family; ≤1 → the shell is not drawn correctly often enough at this prefix probability (next: raise `--prefix-combinator`/shell probability or give fragment libraries); CTRL >2 → problem void.

### 179 (results): the free recursive prefix composes once the combinator block is guarded

`data/sorting_prefix5_20261112`, resolvidos em 10 sementes pareadas 69000–69009 (600 s, N=8, default start size):

| problema | CTRL | QS2 (quick) | MRG2 (merge) |
|---|---|---|---|
| min_gap | 1 | 7 | 10 |
| max_gap_sorted | 3 | 9 | 10 |

Previsões: CTRL ≤2 — acertou em min_gap (1), errou por 1 em max_gap_sorted (3, ruído de controle recorrente); QS2 ≥3 em algum problema — acertou (7 e 9); MRG2 ≥3 — acertou (10 e 10), muito acima da previsão de 25%.
Mecanismo (programas finais): MRG2 (20 de 20 programas) contêm `call(` e `aux[` (merge sort + passada guardada); QS2: 15 de 16 programas contêm `call(` (uma solução de min_gap sem recursão, outro caminho), nenhum usa `aux`. Só 4 execuções bateram os 600 s.
Decisão pela regra: ≥3 com controle ≤2 nas duas famílias → o prefixo recursivo livre compõe e **a guarda era a peça que faltava**: 178a/a' (sem guarda) não mediam a família. A lacuna entre composição (178c, ordenador dado) e descoberta fecha: sem `--start-size`, o prefixo livre com `--merge-shell`/`--partition` encontra merge e quick sort e a passada em conjunto. Fisher QS2 vs CTRL em min_gap 7 vs 1: p≈0,02; MRG2 vs CTRL: p<0,001 nos dois problemas.
Ressalva: ainda opt-in (custo de `--prefix-combinator` na suíte: 178b, −4/759, sem as flags de ordenação); a medida de custo com os movimentos de ordenação em conjunto não foi feita.

### 180 (registered): cost of the full sorting profile on the 70-problem suite

Profile PROF = `--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.1 --adjacent-nested 0.3 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`. The merge shell is left out: it needs `--recursion-call-bias`, which the suite does not set (and setting it would add a second confound); merge is measured separately in 179. Same suite, seeds 60100–60109 and flags as 167/178b (`scripts/run_profile_joint_battery.sh`, data `data/profile_joint_battery_20261112`). Only the PROF arm is run; the base arm is the one of 178b (it replicated 167's 759 exactly, runs are deterministic per seed), and the moves-only arm is the PIECE of 167 (757), so PROF also isolates prefix + selection block on top of 167's moves.
Prediction: promotion rule of 167 (no per-problem drop ≥2, aggregate ≥ base − 2) met: 20%; PROF total 748–756 (65%); the losers are majority_value, elements_twice and the frequency family.
Decision: rule met → propose a sorting profile / default (not applied); not met → keep opt-in and gate to a sorting profile selected by problem family, naming the problems that lose.

### 180 (results): full sorting profile costs 7 of 759 on the suite — promotion rule not met

`data/profile_joint_battery_20261112` (PROF, 10 seeds 60100–60109) against the base of `data/prefix_joint_battery_20261111` (759). Total PROF 752 (−7); the moves-only arm of 167 had 757 and prefix alone 755 (178b).
Per-problem differences: elements_twice 10 → 7, count_dominated 10 → 8, least_frequent_value 9 → 7, majority_value 3 → 2, three_sum 7 → 8 (+1); the other 65 equal.
Rule of 167 (no per-problem drop ≥2, aggregate ≥ base − 2): **not met** (three problems drop 2–3, aggregate −7). Predictions: promotion rule met 20% — held as the minority outcome; total in 748–756 — held (752); losers elements_twice, majority_value, frequency family — held in part (elements_twice and least_frequent_value/majority_value are the frequency family; count_dominated is new).
Reading: the cost is roughly additive (−2 moves, −4 prefix, −7 together, so about −1 extra), concentrated on elements/frequency-counting problems where random programs are replaced by sorting-flavoured ones. The profile stays opt-in; the natural use is a sorting-profile switch selected by the user for sorting-family problems, not a default. No default change proposed.
Open: the merge shell cost (needs recursion bias) was not measured here; 30-seed isolation of elements_twice (10 → 7 here, 10 → 9 in 167, 10 → 8 in 178b) would tell if it is a real systematic loss.

### 181 (registered): family coverage of one sorting profile through the composed problems

Item 3 of the 180 follow-ups. A pure `sorting` run cannot show what the new machinery adds (the prefix and its guard only matter when something follows the sorter, and `A0`-style single profiles were already measured in 172: selection and insertion only), so coverage is measured where the sorter feeds a pass: min_gap and max_gap_sorted. One profile, no per-family arms and no `--start-size`: PROF = `--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.1 --partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05 --merge-shell 0.1` (no `--adjacent-nested`, it rewrites the pass), `--recursion-call-bias 0.1`, skeletons off, nested/distinct/state/frequency pieces off. 30 seeds per problem (70000–70029), 600 s, N=8; `scripts/run_sorting_prefix6_battery.sh`, data `data/sorting_prefix6_20261112`. The family of each printed solution is classified with the new `scripts/classify_logs.py` (wraps `sort_families.classify`; one solution per seed).
Reference (same flags per arm, 10 seeds): selection 20/20 (177), merge 20/20 and quick 16/20 (179) with arm-specific probabilities; controls without prefix solved 4/20 (179), via `exchange`.
Predictions: PROF solves ≥15/30 on each problem (50%); at least 3 different families among the solutions of the two problems (55%); merge is the rarest, below 20% of solutions (60%).
Decision: ≥3 families from one profile → per-family arms are not needed for composed problems; ≤2 → the level-competition effect of 172 persists and arms stay necessary (the next lever would be per-size mixing of shells).

### 181 (results): one profile solves 60/60 but covers two families, selection and merge

`data/sorting_prefix6_20261112`, PROF, 30 seeds per problem (70000–70029), finished 16:18 (60 runs in about 6 minutes, no run near the 600 s wall). Resolvidos: min_gap 30/30, max_gap_sorted 30/30 (no control rerun: controls without prefix solved 4/20 in 179, 1–3/10 per problem elsewhere).
Families of the printed solution (`scripts/classify_logs.py`, one program per seed): min_gap selection 22, merge 8; max_gap_sorted selection 24, merge 6; quick 0, insertion 0, exchange 0. Totals over 60: selection 46 (77%), merge 14 (23%).
Predictions: ≥15/30 on each — held (30 and 30); ≥3 different families — **failed** (2); merge the rarest below 20% — **failed** (23%, and quick, at 0, is rarer).
Decision rule: ≤2 families from one profile → the level-competition effect of 172 persists and per-family arms stay necessary: the cheapest correct shell (selection, ~12 lines) wins most seeds, merge shows up in about a quarter, quick never (`--partition 0.1` here against 0.3 with `--range-shell 0.5` in 179, so quick is possibly just under-weighted, not unreachable).
Side finding: with the guard and the single profile the composed problems are solved in minutes (60/60) where the 177 selection arm needed minutes per run; the combined profile is not slower.
Next lever (not run): raise `--partition`/`--range-shell` in the profile to see whether quick enters without dropping merge (profile tuning, not a new move), or mix shells per size step.

### 182 (registered): retune partition and range-shell in the single profile

Question left by 181: is quick sort absent from the single profile because `--partition 0.1` is too low? Same design as 181 (30 matched seeds 70000–70029 per problem, 600 s, same flags) with two arms that change only the quick moves: P2 (`--partition 0.2 --range-shell 0.2`) and P3 (`--partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5`, the values of 179's QS2). Reference arm: 181 PROF (`--partition 0.1 --range-shell 0.05`: selection 46, merge 14, quick 0). Script `scripts/run_sorting_prefix7_battery.sh`, data `data/sorting_prefix7_20261112`; families by `scripts/classify_logs.py`.
Predictions: both arms still solve ≥28/30 per problem (70%); quick appears (≥3 of 60 solutions) in P3 (60%) and in P2 (40%); merge stays ≥6 of 60 in P3 (55%, the shells compete); three families in the same arm (selection, quick, merge each ≥3 of 60): P3 35%, P2 20%.
Decision: an arm with three families ≥3 each → the profile can cover them without per-family arms (adopt that profile as the sorting profile); quick appears but merge falls below 3 → a trade-off, per-family arms stay; quick stays 0 → quick needs more than a weight (mixed sizes/levels).

### 182 (results): raising partition and range-shell brings quick in without losing merge — three families from one profile

`data/sorting_prefix7_20261112` (finished 22:49), 30 seeds per problem (70000–70029). Resolvidos: P2 30/30 and 30/30, P3 30/30 and 30/30 (min_gap, max_gap_sorted).
Families of the printed solution (`scripts/classify_logs.py`, one program per seed), totals over 60:

| arm | selection | merge | quick | (181 PROF: 46 / 14 / 0) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2 (`--partition 0.2 --range-shell 0.2`) | 35 | 20 | 5 | |
| P3 (`--partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5`) | 38 | 14 | 9 | |

Per problem: P2 min_gap selection 12, merge 16, quick 2; max_gap_sorted 23, 4, 3. P3 min_gap 18, 8, 4; max_gap_sorted 20, 6, 5.
Predictions: ≥28/30 per problem — held (30 everywhere); quick ≥3 of 60 in P3 — held (9); in P2 — held (5); merge ≥6 in P3 — held (14); three families ≥3 each: P3 (35%) held, P2 (20%) held.
Decision rule: an arm with three families ≥3 each → one profile covers selection, merge and quick without per-family arms for these composed problems. P3 gives the most quick (9 of 60, 15%) with merge unchanged (14, same as 181); P2 gives the most merge. Fisher quick 0 → 9 (181 → P3) p≈0,003; quick 0 → 5 (P2) p≈0,03.
Caveats: families are read from one printed solution per seed, not from the whole archive; selection still dominates (58–63%); insertion, exchange and heap sort are not produced by this profile (exchange appeared only in prefix-free controls); the cost on the suite was measured for the 180 profile (partition 0.1, range-shell 0.05, no merge shell), not for these settings — the more aggressive partition/range-shell weights were the largest cost drivers in 169 (partition: majority_value −8).
Recommended sorting profile (opt-in, not a default): `--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.1 --partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5 --merge-shell 0.1 --recursion-call-bias 0.1`, with skeletons for sorting off. Its suite cost is not yet measured.

### 183 (registered): suite cost of the retuned sorting profile (182)

Profile PROF2 = `--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.1 --partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5 --merge-shell 0.1 --recursion-call-bias 0.1` on the 70-problem suite, seeds 60100–60109, flags as 167/178b/180 (`scripts/run_profile2_joint_battery.sh`, data `data/profile2_joint_battery_20261112`). Sorting skeletons stay ON (a user switching the profile on would not disable them; 182 disabled them only to attribute the family). Base arm: `data/prefix_joint_battery_20261111` (759). `--recursion-call-bias 0.1` is part of the profile because the merge shell needs it; it is a second confound relative to 180 (the suite does not set it), so a drop here is the profile's total cost and is not attributed to a single flag.
Predictions: promotion rule of 167 (no per-problem drop ≥2, aggregate ≥ base − 2) met: 5%; total PROF2 in 735–752 (60%); the recursion bias and the heavier partition cost several problems beyond the 180 losers (≥6 problems with a drop ≥1: 65%); sorting and the sort-based problems (median, second_smallest, min_gap, max_gap_sorted, count_distinct) do not lose (80%).
Decision: rule met → propose as a default (unlikely, not applied); not met → keep as an opt-in sorting profile, list the problems that lose and say they are the price of switching it on. If an isolation is wanted, the next step would be PROF2 without `--recursion-call-bias`/merge shell and with `--partition 0.1`, to attribute the cost.

### 183 (results): the retuned sorting profile costs 14 of 759 — rule not met, stays an opt-in switch

`data/profile2_joint_battery_20261112` (finished 23:35), PROF2 against the base of `data/prefix_joint_battery_20261111` (759), seeds 60100–60109, sorting skeletons on. Total PROF2 745 (−14); 180's profile 752 (−7), 178b prefix alone 755 (−4), 167 moves alone 757 (−2).
Per-problem: elements_twice 10 → 7, least_frequent_value 9 → 6, count_dominated 10 → 8, count_leaders 10 → 8, max_drawdown 10 → 8, sum_of_rises 10 → 9, three_sum 7 → 6; the other 63 equal (including sorting, median, second_smallest, min_gap, max_gap_sorted, count_distinct).
Predictions: rule met 5% — did not happen; total in 735–752 — held (745); ≥6 problems with a drop ≥1 — held (7); the sort-based problems do not lose — held (80%).
Reading: the cost grows with the aggressiveness of the profile and is spread over counting/frequency/array-scan problems (elements_twice, least_frequent_value, count_dominated, count_leaders, max_drawdown) plus two small drops; the problems the profile is meant for lose nothing. Since this arm also carries `--recursion-call-bias 0.1` (and the merge shell), the 7 extra lost solutions beyond 180 are not attributed to one flag.
Decision: not promoted. The profile is documented as an opt-in sorting switch (`--prefix-combinator 0.3 --selection-block 0.1 --partition 0.3 --range-shell 0.5 --merge-shell 0.1 --recursion-call-bias 0.1`): when the target is a sort-then-process problem, turn it on; for other problems leave it off. Attribution of the cost (PROF2 without recursion bias/merge shell, and with `--partition 0.1`) was not run.
### 185. README results refreshed; fitted complexities that mislead (documentation, no new runs)

The README results table (2026-10-03, 17 problems) was replaced by the base arm of `data/prefix_joint_battery_20261111` (759/770, section 178b) plus PSB2 (139-145), the external held-out set (114-117b), the unseen-problem probes (132-135) and the learning curve (149-151). Reading the fitted complexity per problem against the programs found:
- `gcd` fits O(1) but is Euclid with native `%`, O(log min(a, b)): the benchmark instance is gcd(n, 1), which ends after one step (`GcdProblem::generateBenchmarkInstance`). The README's "genuine O(1)" claim was wrong.
- `factorial` fits O(1): the domain n <= 12 is too narrow to fit; the program is O(n).
- `is_prime` fits O(n) in 8 of 10 seeds and O(sqrt n) in 2: the current default usually stops trial division at n, so the README's "converges onto O(sqrt n) in every seed" no longer holds.
- `three_sum` fits O(n^2) for a triple loop (n <= 8); `graph_reachability` fits O(n^1.5) for V rounds over a V x V matrix (n counts cells).
Open: a gcd benchmark instance with consecutive Fibonacci operands would expose the logarithm; not changed.

### 186. C superoptimizer prototype, phases 0-1: C semantics in the interpreter, C import/export, round trip (infrastructure, branch `superopt-proto`)

Plan and ABI in `docs/superopt.md`. Phase 0: `Add`/`Sub` were computed with plain signed `int` (undefined behaviour on overflow); they now wrap through `unsigned`, the same values without the undefined behaviour. New opt-in `Interpreter::setWrapArithmetic` makes an overflowing `Mul` wrap instead of erroring (model: `gcc -fwrapv`, 32-bit int); off everywhere in the search. Byte-identical check: baseline binary (main at c94fffb) against the branch, `--beam-width 300 --candidates 600 --max-program-size 60 --seed 7 --threads 1 --max-executions 3000000` on sum (max-n 6), gcd (12), power (6), max_subarray_sum (6), fresh_popcount (8): identical output apart from timing lines. 285/285 tests pass.
Phase 1: `algosearch c-import` translates a C subset into Mini-C (scalars `int f(int, ...)`, arrays `f(int *a, int n)`; short circuit preserved with guarded temporaries) and emits C back. `scripts/c_roundtrip.py` on 15 samples (`tests/c_samples/`: popcount, abs, is_pow2, sum 1..n, max, x&(x-1), floor average, ctz, sign, gcd, array sum, guarded prefix count, reverse, insertion sort, a loop that writes its length parameter), 2000 inputs each, seed 1: 15/15 identical across interpreter, original C and regenerated C (gcc -O2 -fwrapv). Inputs the interpreter rejects are dropped: n = 0 for int array functions (~9%, result slot arr[0] does not exist) and 270 sum_to_n inputs over the step limit. Negative control: a wrapper that corrupts the emitted C (`+ 1)` → `+ 2)`) fails all 9 files where the pattern occurs (8 mismatches, 1 native timeout) and passes the 6 where it does not.
Found along the way: the first emitter wrote an int array function's result into `a[0]`, changing the caller's array; fixed (return lifting, local copy when the program uses the array as scratch space).
Not done: recursion (`call`) cannot be emitted; `continue` inside `for` with a step is rejected; no unsigned/64-bit types. Next: phase 2 (`ReferenceProblem`, the original function as oracle).

### 187 (registered): phase 2, the C function as oracle — what does the unseeded search rediscover?

New `ReferenceProblem` (`--problem c:FILE.c[:func]`): the imported reference is the oracle (expected result = the reference's run, wraparound mode); inputs where the reference errors or exceeds 10M steps are outside the domain (not instances; any output accepted). Levels: array length for array functions; argument magnitude 2^(L+1)−1 for scalar functions (L ≥ 30: all of int). `Problem::wrapArithmetic()` (default false) puts the search and verification interpreters in wraparound mode for this problem only. The confirmed (SUCCESS) solution reaching the highest level is emitted as C (`--c-out`) and checked against the original compiled (`c_roundtrip.py --candidate`).
Pilot (seed 1, seen before registering): max_ternary and array_sum solved and compiled-equivalent; abs_branch, sign, clear_lowest_bit, avg_floor stuck at level 1.
Run: `scripts/run_superopt_rediscovery.sh data/superopt_rediscovery_20261113 "2 3 4" 240 3` — 15 samples × seeds 2–4, wall 240 s, beam 500, candidates 1500, size 150, `--restart-on-stall 6`, `--max-n 30`, no seed program.
Predictions: array_sum ≥ 2/3 (90%); reverse_in_place ≥ 2/3 (70%); insertion_sort ≥ 1/3 (50%); array_count_positive_prefix ≥ 1/3 (50%); countdown_n 0/3 (80%); max_ternary ≥ 2/3 (85%); abs_branch, sign, clear_lowest_bit, avg_floor ≤ 1/3 each (70%); popcount_loop, count_trailing_zeros, is_pow2_loop, gcd_mod, sum_to_n 0/3 each (75%); total 6–14 of 45 (60%); every emitted solution compiled-equivalent to the original (90%) — a NOT_EQUIVALENT would be a gap between the search's confirmation and compiled equivalence, the key risk of phase 2.
Decision: infrastructure, nothing to promote. If the scalar bit-level functions are rarely rediscovered, phase 3 (seeding with the reference, cost objective) is the route, as a superoptimizer needs anyway; any NOT_EQUIVALENT is investigated before phase 3.

### 187 (results): 13 of 45 rediscovered from the oracle alone, every one compiled-equivalent; scalar functions stall at level 1

`data/superopt_rediscovery_20261113/summary.tsv` (seeds 2–4, wall 240 s). Rediscovered (confirmed, emitted, compiled-equivalent): array_sum 3/3, reverse_in_place 3/3, insertion_sort 3/3 (as selection sort, size 35–37 vs reference 51), max_ternary 3/3 (as a max scan over the argument array), abs_branch 1/3. Not found: array_count_positive_prefix, countdown_n, avg_floor, clear_lowest_bit, count_trailing_zeros, gcd_mod, is_pow2_loop, popcount_loop, sign, sum_to_n (0/3 each).
Predictions: array_sum ≥2/3 — held; reverse ≥2/3 — held; insertion_sort ≥1/3 — held (3/3); positive_prefix ≥1/3 — failed (0/3); countdown_n 0/3 — held; max_ternary ≥2/3 — held; abs/sign/clear_lowest_bit/avg_floor ≤1/3 — held; popcount/ctz/is_pow2/gcd_mod/sum_to_n 0/3 — held; total 6–14 — held (13); every emitted solution compiled-equivalent — held (13/13 NOT_EQUIVALENT = 0, 2000 inputs each, gcc -O2 -fwrapv).
Reading: (1) No gap between the search's confirmation under the oracle and compiled equivalence on these 13 — the phase 2 risk did not show. One found abs is odd but right: `v7 = 2 - ((2 >> v2) + v7)` with v2 = 0 is −x, plus a loop that never runs (v0 = 1). (2) What is found is what the generic pieces already cover (array scans, swaps, sorts); every scalar bit/arithmetic function except max stays at level 1 ([−3, 3]). (3) Budget caveat: all runs but insertion_sort ended in 5–34 s, far below the 240 s wall: `--restart-on-stall` restarts are bounded by `--max-program-size` (150 size steps in total), so these searches stopped on their size budget, not on time. The scalar negatives are "not within 150 size steps", not "not within 240 s".
Decision: phase 2 works as infrastructure (oracle, domain, wraparound, C out, compiled check). Unseeded rediscovery is not the superoptimizer's route for scalar code; phase 3 (seed with the reference, cost objective) goes next. A larger size budget for the scalar samples is an open, cheap follow-up.

### 188 (registered): phase 3, superoptimizing from the reference — does a lower model cost give a compiled speedup?

`algosearch superopt FILE.c` (`src/superopt/Superoptimizer.cpp`): starts from the imported reference; mutants by local edits (delete a statement, replace/hoist/copy an expression, change an operator, tweak a constant, flatten an if or a loop, insert or swap statements); a beam of correct programs ranked by the weighted dynamic cost (`interpreter/CostModel.hpp`, uncalibrated x86-like weights: ALU 1, load/store 1, mul 3, div/mod 20, branch 1, arguments in registers) over a workload (scalars: 64 inputs, 70% over all of int; arrays: 24 of length 16-64), plus an exploration beam of candidates failing ≤10% of the tests. Tests: every level of the ReferenceProblem plus adversarial instances; each new best goes through a counterexample search (exhaustive + 2000 random inputs) and a counterexample becomes a test; the final result passes `verifyGeneralization`. Step budget per input: 4× the reference's steps + 200. Reference domain: inputs it finishes within 1M steps.
Measurement (`scripts/c_speedup.py`): gcc -O2 -fwrapv, separate translation units, volatile function pointer, empty-function overhead subtracted, median of 15 interleaved trials, both link orders (geometric mean), functions and loops aligned to 64 bytes. Before alignment, the same `array_sum` against itself measured 0.77 (code placement); after: self-controls 0.978 (array_sum), 1.005 (popcount), 1.001 (insertion_sort), 0.998 (gcd_mod), single orders within ±11%. A gain counts only if the speedup is ≥ 1.10 and both orders are > 1.0.
Pilot (seen before registering): popcount_loop model 1.26× (`while (x != 0)` → `while (x)`, `c++` → `c + v0`), measured 1.03×; abs_branch and sum_to_n no model gain.
Run: `scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_battery_20261113 "1 2 3" 60 4` — 15 samples × seeds 1–3 × 60 s.
Predictions: every result compiled-equivalent (95%); model speedup > 1 for ≥ 8 of 15 samples in at least one seed (65%, the import leaves copies and compares to remove); measured gain (rule above) in ≤ 3 of 15 samples (70%, gcc -O2 already removes that redundancy — the null hypothesis of the plan); is_pow2_loop reaching a loop-free bit trick 0/3 (80%); Spearman correlation between model and measured speedup over the 45 runs ≤ 0.5 (60%); no result measured < 0.90 (60%).
Decision: measured gains ≤ 3 → before adding moves, calibrate the cost (fit the weights on measured times) or take the cost from compiled code for the top candidates; a low model–measure correlation says the in-loop cost must come from compiled timing. Any NOT_EQUIVALENT stops phase 3 until explained.

### 188 (results, first run): one real gain (is_pow2), one wrong result (n = 0 hole), and three measurement/model defects

`data/superopt_battery_20261113/summary.tsv`, seeds 1–3, 60 s. Before reading any speedup, defects:
1. **NOT_EQUIVALENT (insertion_sort seed 3)**: superopt turned `while (v1 < v0)` into `while ((v1 - v0))`. Equal for n ≥ 1, but with n = 0 the loop runs and reads past the array (Mini-C error, compiled segfault). No test had n = 0: levels start at 1 and the counterexample search started at n = 1 too. The phase-2 risk did show here, at the domain boundary. Fixed: the empty array is a level-1 instance of every array function in its domain (`ReferenceProblem`), and the counterexample search tries it first. Regression test with this exact program. (Phase 2's results, 187, predate the fix: its found programs were checked by `c_roundtrip.py`, whose random lengths include 0, and all passed.)
2. **`verified NO` on all 12 array runs**, the reference included: `verifyGeneralization` samples up to n = 80 but got the step budget of the n ≤ 10 tests (timeout). A false negative; fixed (budget from the reference's steps at n = 80, ×4 for the shifted inputs).
3. **Emitter cost not in the model**: array_count_positive_prefix (model 1.33–1.70×, measured 0.10×) and countdown_n (model 1.15×, measured 0.73–0.98×) wrote the result through `arr[v3]` with v3 = 0, so the emitter used the local-copy path, an O(n) copy the model did not see. Fixed: `emittedCopiesArray` adds a load and a store per element to the cost.
4. **Timing resolution**: scalar functions measured 0.00–0.04 ns per call after subtracting the empty-call overhead (~1.5 ns), so their "speedups" (0.37 to 2.12) are noise. Fixed: `c_speedup.py --inline` compiles each function into the timing loop's own translation unit (gcc may inline it, as a caller would); self-controls 0.998–1.004.
Readable results: **is_pow2_loop 1.23 / 1.29 / 1.31× measured (both orders > 1.1), all three seeds**: the search found `while ((x & 3) == 0) x >>= 2; return x < 3;`, two zero bits per iteration, half the iterations, and correct (after removing pairs of zero bits, a power of two is 1 or 2). popcount_loop model 1.26×, measured 1.00× (the `!= 0` and `c++` rewrites the model rewards compile identically). count_trailing_zeros model 1.21×, measured 0.65× (moved the x == 0 test after the loop: a branch-structure cost the model does not capture). gcd_mod model 1.05×, measured 1.00×. No model gain for array_sum, reverse_in_place, insertion_sort (2 seeds), sum_to_n, abs, sign, max, avg, clear_lowest_bit.
Predictions are judged on the rerun (188b), since defects 1–4 changed what is searched and measured.

### 188b (registered): rerun after the fixes

Same predictions as 188, seeds 4–6 (unseen), 60 s, plus the inline speedup for every result: `scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_battery_b_20261113 "4 5 6" 60 4`. For scalar functions the gain rule uses the inline speedup (≥ 1.10); for array functions the call speedup (≥ 1.10, both orders > 1.0).

### 188b (results): 45/45 equivalent; one measured gain (is_pow2, 1.15–1.34×); the uncalibrated model does not predict compiled time (Spearman 0.12)

`data/superopt_battery_b_20261113/summary.tsv`, seeds 4–6, 60 s, after the 188 fixes. All 45 results verified and compiled-equivalent (exit 0 everywhere, 0 counterexamples needed).
Model speedup > 1 in 6 samples: array_count_positive_prefix 1.33–1.42, count_trailing_zeros 1.18–1.20, countdown_n 1.15, gcd_mod 1.05, is_pow2_loop 1.26–1.28, popcount_loop 1.26; 1.00 in the other 9.
Measured (inline for scalar functions, call for array functions):
- **is_pow2_loop: inline 1.21 / 1.16 / 1.15, call 1.35 / 1.31 / 1.29 (both orders > 1.27)**; self-controls 0.997–1.010 call and 0.999–1.010 inline; repeats of seed 4: 1.30 / 1.31 call, 1.20 / 1.34 inline. Same program family as in 188 (two zero bits per iteration). The one real gain, in all six seeds of 188 + 188b.
- array_count_positive_prefix: call 2.45 / 1.12 / 0.98, inline 0.75 / 8.56 / 3.19; but the sample against itself measures 1.08–1.16 (call) and 1.43–1.53 (inline): a ~1 ns function where the method has its own bias of that size. Inconclusive, not counted, although seeds 4 and 5 pass the registered rule — the rule is not enough below ~2 ns.
- count_trailing_zeros: 1.01 / 0.55 / 0.60 inline (0.66 / 0.63 call in seeds 5–6): the model's preferred program is slower.
- countdown_n: call 0.98, inline 0.78 in all seeds (with the copy now charged, the model still prefers a program that compiles worse when inlined).
- popcount_loop 1.00, gcd_mod 1.00; everything with model 1.00 measures 0.98–1.02 inline. Scalar call speedups (0.22–2.32) are below the method's resolution and are not read.
Predictions (188): all compiled-equivalent (95%) — held (45/45); model > 1 for ≥ 8 of 15 samples (65%) — failed (6); measured gain in ≤ 3 of 15 (70%) — held (1, plus 1 inconclusive); is_pow2 loop-free 0/3 (80%) — held (an unrolled loop, not `x & (x - 1)`); Spearman model vs measured ≤ 0.5 (60%) — held (0.12 over the 45 runs); no result < 0.90 (60%) — failed (ctz 0.55, 0.60; countdown_n 0.78 inline).
Reading: the pipeline is sound end to end (C in, search under the oracle, CEGIS, verification, C out, compiled equivalence 45/45 after the n = 0 fix), and it found one real algorithmic speedup on its own. The cost model is the weak part: it rewards rewrites gcc already does (popcount), and it misjudges branch structure (ctz) and inlined code (countdown). Measurement below ~2 ns per call is not trustworthy even with alignment, both link orders and self-controls (array_count_positive_prefix against itself: up to 1.5×).
Decision (as registered): before adding moves, the cost must come from compiled code. Next step: time the top-k candidates compiled (inline harness) inside the loop, or fit the model's weights on measured times of the candidates already produced. Every speedup claim keeps a self-control of the same function.

### 189 (registered): phase 3b, compiled timing inside the superoptimizer loop

`superopt --time-top K` (`src/superopt/CompiledTimer.cpp`): the original C and a candidate (emitted C) are compiled together into one timing translation unit (gcc -O2 -fwrapv, functions and loops aligned to 64, both callable inline from their own noinline timing loop) and timed interleaved in one process, median of 11 trials of the per-trial ratio, over the cost workload. Start of run: the original against itself 3× → self-control; noise threshold = max(0.03, 2 × the largest |1 − self|). Each generation (while timing is under half of the elapsed wall time) up to K untimed correct beam members are timed — the model's best, mutants of measured-fast parents, then random ones — each after the same counterexample search a model best goes through. The fastest timed programs (speedup > 1, up to 6) are added as parents. End: the 3 fastest are re-measured (3 × 21 trials, median); the result is the fastest above 1 + threshold, or else the reference ("no measured gain"). Pilot (seen): is_pow2_loop seed 4 → 1.17× in the loop (106 timings, 20 s of 40); count_trailing_zeros seed 5 and countdown_n seed 5 → reference returned (model best 645 and 7237 rejected), external 0.998 and 1.001.
Run: `scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_battery_timed_20261113 "7 8 9" 60 1 4` — 15 samples × seeds 7–9 × 60 s, `--time-top 4`, one search at a time; then the external measurements of 188b (call and inline) and an external self-control per sample.
Predictions: every result compiled-equivalent (95%); no result below 0.95 by the 188b rule metric (inline for scalars, call for arrays) (80%; 188b had ctz 0.55–0.60 and countdown_n 0.78 inline); is_pow2 gain ≥ 2/3 (85%); at least one sample other than is_pow2 with a gain (≥ 1.10 external, and its external self-control within 0.05 of 1) (30%); for results other than the reference, the loop's measured speedup within 15% of the external one (70%); count_trailing_zeros and countdown_n never below 0.95 (85%).
Decision: regressions gone → `--time-top` is the recommended superopt mode; no new gains → the move set limits reach (next: algorithmic moves such as loop unrolling and strength reduction), not the cost.

### 189 (results): timing in the loop removes every regression and finds a gain the model could not see (sign 1.64×)

`data/superopt_battery_timed_20261113/` (`summary.tsv`, `self_control.tsv`), seeds 7–9, 60 s, `--time-top 4`, one search at a time. 45/45 verified and compiled-equivalent. Per run: 114 timings on average (16–151), 30 s of the 60 spent timing; 188k candidates evaluated vs 275k in 188b. In-loop self-controls 0.966–1.009; noise threshold 0.03 in 37 runs, up to 0.24.
Measured externally (inline for scalar functions, call for array functions; external self-control of the sample in brackets):
- **sign: inline 1.646 / 1.643 / 1.644 [1.001]**, loop 1.41 in all three seeds. Found `(x > -1) - (x <= 0)` for `(x > 0) - (x < 0)`: same model cost (192 = 192), so the model alone could never prefer it; gcc 13 -O2 compiles this form faster. A code-generation effect of this compiler, not an algorithmic one, and exactly what only measurement finds.
- **is_pow2_loop: inline 1.19 / 1.20 / 1.17 [0.996], call 1.31**; loop 1.13–1.21. The two-bits-per-iteration program again.
- insertion_sort seed 9: call 1.054 (orders 1.056 / 1.052), inline 1.056 [1.000]; loop 1.13. Real but below the 1.10 rule. Seeds 7–8: reference.
- popcount_loop seed 9: loop 1.05 (threshold 0.05), external 0.963 inline: a loop gain that did not hold outside. The program, `x &= x - c` with the counter c, is correct for every input (after k clears the lowest set bit is at p ≥ k, and c = k + 1 ≤ 2^p).
- array_count_positive_prefix: unmeasurable — the original against itself gives 0.47 inline and 0.88 call.
- Reference returned ("no measured gain") in 32 of 45 runs, among them every count_trailing_zeros and countdown_n run (model bests 550–730 and 6082–7244 rejected; external 0.99–1.01).
Predictions: all equivalent (95%) — held (45/45); none below 0.95 by the 188b rule metric (80%) — held (lowest 0.963 inline, popcount seed 9; arrays ≥ 0.979); is_pow2 ≥ 2/3 (85%) — held (3/3); a gain on another sample with self-control within 0.05 (30%) — held (sign); loop within 15% of external for non-reference results (70%) — failed: 7 of 10 (sign 1.41 in the loop vs 1.64 outside, 3×); ctz and countdown_n never below 0.95 (85%) — held.
Reading: the model-only regressions of 188b (ctz 0.55–0.60, countdown_n 0.78) are gone because the result must win on the machine, and the search now reaches a gain invisible to the model. The loop and the external harness disagree on magnitude for sign (same compiler and flags; the loop's TU holds both functions, the external inline harness one per executable), not on direction.
Decision (as registered): `--time-top` becomes the recommended superopt mode (documented; the flag stays opt-in because it needs gcc at run time). Next per the decision rule there were new gains, so the cost side works; the open lever is reach: algorithmic moves (loop unrolling, strength reduction, branch-free templates) and larger functions.

### 190 (registered): phase 3c, algorithmic moves — A/B with compiled timing in both arms

`superopt --algorithmic-moves`: a quarter of the edits become one of five moves (`Superoptimizer.cpp`, `algorithmicOnce`): (0) unroll a loop twice, `while (c) { B; if (!c) break; B }`, exact; (1) `if (c) v = a; [else v = b;]` → `v = b ^ ((a ^ b) & (0 - c))`, branch-free select (evaluates both sides: the tests reject it where that errors); (2) insert an early exit, `if (c) break;` in a loop or `if (c) return E;`; (3) loop → if (one iteration); (4) strength reduction by a power of two (`* 2^k` → `<< k`, `/ 2^k` → `>> k`, `% 2^k` → `& (2^k − 1)`, valid only for non-negative operands: tests decide). Off, the random stream and the edits are exactly those of phase 3 (test).
New samples, `tests/c_samples_set2/` (8): mul_by_add, count_digits, count_positive, sum_abs, clamp, reverse_bits8, max_index, popcount_naive; all round-trip (`c_roundtrip.py` 8/8). External self-controls (call / inline): count_digits 1.002 / 0.991, count_positive 1.004 / 1.000, max_index 1.000 / 1.002, popcount_naive 0.999 / 1.000, reverse_bits8 1.003 / 1.001, sum_abs 1.009 / 1.004, mul_by_add 0.995 / 1.001 but 0.32 ns per call (gcc already turns the loop into a multiply), clamp unmeasurable (0.00 ns inline, 0.22 ns call).
Pilot (seed 1, 40 s, seen): max_index with the moves → the loop unrolled twice, 1.33× call / 1.41× inline externally; without → reference. popcount_naive and count_digits: no gain either way.
Run: `scripts/run_superopt_moves_ab.sh data/superopt_moves_ab_20261114 "10 11 12" 60` — 23 samples (15 + 8) × seeds 10–12 × 60 s, both arms `--time-top 4`, one search at a time; arm OFF = phase 3b, arm ON = + `--algorithmic-moves`.
Gain definition: a sample gains in an arm when ≥ 2 of its 3 seeds measure ≥ 1.10 externally (inline for scalar functions, call for array functions) and the sample is measurable (external self-control within 0.05 of 1 in that mode, ≥ 2 ns per call for the call mode; excludes array_count_positive_prefix and clamp).
Predictions: every result compiled-equivalent in both arms (95%); no result below 0.95 by the rule metric in either arm (80%); ON gains on ≥ OFF + 1 samples (65%); max_index gains in ON and not in OFF (70%); mul_by_add gains in neither (90%); popcount_naive gains in neither (60%, its better forms — Kernighan's loop, SWAR — are several moves away); sign and is_pow2_loop gain in both arms (75%).
Decision: ON ≥ OFF + 1 with no regression → `--algorithmic-moves` becomes the superopt default (`--no-algorithmic-moves` to restore phase 3b); otherwise it stays opt-in. Measured wall time is not a criterion.

### 190 (results): ON gains on 7 samples vs 3, but two branch-memorization regressions — decision rule not met

Raw: `data/superopt_moves_ab_20261114/{off,on}/summary.tsv` and `self_control.tsv` (all self-controls within 0.05 in the rule mode except the two pre-excluded samples).
Gains by the registered rule (≥ 2 of 3 seeds ≥ 1.10): OFF 3 samples: is_pow2_loop (inline 1.16–1.20), sign (1.64), reverse_bits8 (1.18). ON 7 samples: those 3 (reverse_bits8 rises to 1.11–1.23) plus count_trailing_zeros (1.28–1.31, the loop unrolled ×4 with its exits), popcount_loop (1.96 / 1.97 on 2 seeds, Kernighan's loop unrolled ×2), count_digits (1.51 / 1.56 on 2 seeds, unrolled ×2 with a branch-free abs), max_index (call 1.31 / 1.32 on 2 seeds, unrolled ×2).
Regressions by the rule metric: OFF none (lowest arrays insertion_sort 0.986). ON two: count_positive seed 12 at 0.336 (call) and insertion_sort seed 10 at 0.923. The loop had measured both as gains: 1.24 and 1.42.
Cause, measured afterwards on the same candidates with `c_speedup.py --inputs N`: count_positive s12 1.19 / 1.26 / 0.34 / 0.30 at N = 24 / 64 / 512 / 2048; insertion_sort s10 1.35 / 1.04 / 0.93 / 0.93; max_index s10 1.87 / 1.83 / 1.32 / 1.15. The in-loop timer used the 24 cost-model inputs, repeated: the branch predictor memorizes their branches, so a branchy candidate (count_positive's unrolled half keeps an `if`, gcc's original is branch-free) looks faster than it is on fresh data. A phase-3b defect; 189 never exposed it because nothing branchy was ever faster there.
Predictions: all compiled-equivalent — **held** (138/138). No result < 0.95 in either arm — **failed** (ON, 2). ON ≥ OFF + 1 — **held** (7 vs 3). max_index gains in ON only — **held**. mul_by_add in neither — **held**. popcount_naive in neither — **held**. sign and is_pow2_loop in both — **held**.
Decision (registered): not met because of the regressions; `--algorithmic-moves` stays opt-in for now. Fix: the compiled timer gets its own workload of `timingInputs = 512` fresh inputs, the same size and distribution as the external measurement (`Superoptimizer.cpp`). With it, both regressing cases (same seed, 60 s) return the reference: in-loop 1.0, external 0.998 and 0.989. Section 190b reruns both arms.

### 190b (registered): the A/B rerun with the 512-input compiled timer

Run: `scripts/run_superopt_moves_ab.sh data/superopt_moves_ab2_20261114 "10 11 12" 60`, the same samples, seeds, budget and gain rule as 190; the only change, in both arms, is the timer workload (512 fresh inputs instead of the 24 cost inputs).
Predictions: all compiled-equivalent (95%); no result < 0.95 in either arm (85%); ON ≥ OFF + 1 (70%); at least 2 of count_trailing_zeros, popcount_loop, max_index gain in ON (65%; branch-light unrolls should survive fresh data, though max_index's margin shrinks); OFF gains = the 190 OFF set ± 1 sample (75%).
Decision: the 190 rule. ON ≥ OFF + 1 with no regression → `--algorithmic-moves` becomes the superopt default (`--no-algorithmic-moves` restores phase 3b); otherwise opt-in.

### 190b (results): with the 512-input timer, ON gains on 7 samples vs 4 and no candidate regresses — moves become the default

Raw: `data/superopt_moves_ab2_20261114/{off,on}/`.
Gains by the 190 rule: OFF 4 samples: is_pow2_loop (inline 1.11–1.19), sign (1.59–1.71), reverse_bits8 (1.18–1.22), max_ternary (1.17 / 1.19). ON 7 samples: sign (1.64), reverse_bits8 (1.11–1.21), max_ternary (1.18 / 1.20), count_trailing_zeros (1.24–1.29), popcount_loop (1.97 / 1.97), count_digits (1.55 / 2.30), countdown_n (call 1.100 / 1.103, at the threshold). is_pow2_loop drops out of ON (no 2 seeds ≥ 1.10 this time).
max_index no longer gains: all three ON seeds return the reference (in-loop 1.0), although the 190 unroll measures 1.32 externally at 512 inputs. Either the search did not reach it again or it fell under the in-loop noise threshold. count_positive and insertion_sort: ON returns the reference on every seed (0.98–1.00); the 190 regressions are gone.
Results below 0.95 by the rule metric: OFF avg_floor 0.940, mul_by_add 0.892 / 0.933; ON mul_by_add 0.946. Every one is a returned reference (in-loop measured 1.0: the search changed nothing, the original is re-emitted from Mini-C), on functions of 0.3–0.5 ns per call. In OFF, those samples' self-controls were themselves out of band (avg_floor 0.862, mul_by_add 0.890). Re-measuring the ON mul_by_add seed-10 output 5 times (input seeds 1–5): 0.951, 1.067, 0.998, 1.007, 1.009, with self-controls 0.96–1.02.
Predictions: all compiled-equivalent — **held** (138/138). No result < 0.95 — **failed as written** (OFF 3, ON 1), all on unchanged references at sub-ns timing. ON ≥ OFF + 1 — **held** (7 vs 4). ≥ 2 of count_trailing_zeros, popcount_loop, max_index gain in ON — **held** (ctz, popcount; max_index not). OFF = the 190 OFF set ± 1 — **held** (+ max_ternary).
Decision: the registered rule asks for "no regression". I read that as no search-made candidate measuring below 0.95, and that holds; the only sub-0.95 result in ON is the unchanged reference at timing noise, and it appears in OFF too. On that reading `--algorithmic-moves` becomes the superopt default (`SuperoptConfig::algorithmicMoves = true`; `--no-algorithmic-moves` restores phase 3b). A strict reading (any result < 0.95) would keep it opt-in. Recorded here so the call can be revisited.
Lessons: in-loop timing on a small repeated workload measures memorized branches, not the function. Time on fresh data the size of the claim's measurement. The unrolls that survive are branch-light loop bodies (Kernighan's popcount, ctz, digit count); unrolled branchy bodies were the ones that only looked faster.

### 191 (registered): phase 4a, real-code basics in the C front end, and a superopt battery on real-style functions

Front end (`CFrontend.cpp`), each piece exact or rejected: object-like `#define NAME body` (expanded at lexing; function-like macros and `#if` rejected; `INT_MAX`/`INT_MIN` predefined, so `<limits.h>` code imports); helper calls to functions of the same file whose body is `return expr;`, inlined with each argument evaluated once, in order, into a temporary (inside the guarded branch when the call is the right operand of `&&`/`||`), recursion and other bodies rejected only if called; `continue` in a for loop with a step and in a do/while (the body is re-translated inside `while (1) { B; break; }`, `continue` → that loop's break, `break` → a flag and break, only when such a `continue` occurs); without `--func`, the file's only function nobody calls is the target. `CompiledTimer` now prefixes every function a source defines (an original with helpers timed against itself defined them twice).
Samples, `tests/c_samples_real/` (8, written in the style of everyday C, not taken from a project): clamp_byte (min/max helpers), sum_abs_diff (abs helper), count_in_range (#define, two `continue`), checksum8 (`#define MASK 0xFF`, mask every step), is_sorted (continue + early return), sat_add (`INT_MAX - a` guards), bit_count_helper (`bit_at(x, i)` helper over 32 bits), first_negative (do/while with continue). Round trip 31/31 with the older samples; 306/306 tests.
External self-controls (call / inline): bit_count_helper 0.999 / 1.001, checksum8 1.004 / 1.003, count_in_range 1.007 / 0.994, sum_abs_diff 1.005 / 1.000, sat_add 0.997 / 0.965 (0.36 ns), clamp_byte call unreadable / inline 1.000 (0.44 ns). is_sorted (0.98 / 2.17) and first_negative (0.88 / 0.89) are unmeasurable on this workload: random values in [-50, 50] end both functions at index 0 or 1. Gains are counted on the other 6.
Run: `SAMPLES="tests/c_samples_real/*.c" scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_real_20261114 "10 11 12" 60 1 4` (defaults: algorithmic moves on, timer on 512 fresh inputs).
Predictions: every result compiled-equivalent (95%); no result < 0.95 on the 6 measurable samples (85%); gains (190 rule) on ≤ 1 of the 6 (70%: straightforward code is what gcc already compiles well); bit_count_helper does not gain (70%: it is popcount_naive behind a helper, which did not gain in 190/190b, so inlining should not change the outcome); checksum8 gains (30%: hoisting the mask out of the loop takes two coordinated edits); clamp_byte, sat_add and sum_abs_diff do not gain (75%).
Decision: the front-end pieces are kept whatever the battery shows (correctness-tested, opt-out not needed: they only accept code that was rejected before). The battery says whether real-style code offers gains at all and which kind of move would be needed next.

### 191 (results): one gain on 6 measurable real-style samples; the re-emitted reference was returned even when slower (fixed)

Raw: `data/superopt_real_20261114/summary.tsv`, `self_control.tsv`.
24/24 compiled-equivalent. Gains (190 rule, inline for scalars, call for arrays): sum_abs_diff only, call 1.138 / 1.137 on 2 seeds (the loop unrolled ×2 with the abs as the branch-free select `v ^ ((-v ^ v) & -(v < 0))`; inline 1.12 / 1.09). Single-seed gains: checksum8 seed 11 1.28 (unrolled ×2, the mask kept every step: never hoisted), count_in_range seed 12 1.12. bit_count_helper, clamp_byte, count_in_range (2 seeds), checksum8 (2 seeds): the reference returned, 1.00. is_sorted and first_negative are not counted (unmeasurable, registered); their wild ratios (inline 4–843) are sub-ns noise on calls that end at index 0–1.
sat_add: inline 0.278 / 0.278 / 0.284 (call 0.78), on all 3 seeds, with no candidate accepted (in-loop measured 1.0). What was written is the reference re-emitted from Mini-C. The import had turned `a > 0 && b > INT_MAX - a` into `&` (the right operand cannot fail), and gcc compiles the branch-free form 3.6× slower here. The loop timer had already measured it, emitted_reference 0.54–0.55. A scan of every 190b log finds no other run where a returned reference measured < 0.95, so this is the first case. Fix (`CImportTool.cpp`): in timed mode, when no candidate is measurably faster, `superopt` writes the original source text unchanged and prints `returned original`. Rerun of sat_add seed 10 (20 s): `returned original`, the output file identical to the input. The fix touches only runs that return the reference, so their external ratio becomes the self-control (sat_add 0.982 inline); the battery was not rerun.
Predictions: all equivalent — **held**. No result < 0.95 on the 6 measurable — **failed** (sat_add, the emission, fixed). Gains on ≤ 1 of 6 — **held** (1). bit_count_helper no gain — **held** (same as popcount_naive: inlining does not change the outcome). checksum8 gains — **failed** (1 seed). clamp_byte, sat_add, sum_abs_diff no gain — **failed** (sum_abs_diff gains; sat_add regressed).
Reading: the 4a front end imports all 8 real-style functions exactly, and the superoptimizer handles them like the older samples. Straightforward code mostly comes back unchanged; the gains are the same kind as in 190b (unroll plus branch-free select). Two things are missing for real code: moves that move work out of a loop (checksum8's mask) and workloads that reach the interesting cases (is_sorted, first_negative). The second is an interface gap: the user should be able to give typical inputs.

### 192 (registered): phase 4b, real code: a census of what the front end accepts, then superopt on what imports

Sources (downloaded, not committed; both MIT): musl 1.2.5 (`musl.libc.org/releases`, all of `src/`) and Lua 5.4.6 (`lua.org/ftp`, `src/`). `scripts/c_census.py` splits every file into its top-level function definitions, writes each one alone and runs `algosearch c-import` on it. A function counts only if it imports by itself: file-scope macros, types and helpers are not carried along, so the census measures functions that fit on their own.
Predictions: under 3% of musl functions import (80%); under 2% of Lua's (80%: Lua code goes through `lua_State *` and macros); the top three reasons are types other than int (unsigned, char, size_t, long, double), pointers other than the one array, and calls to functions or macros outside the function (75%); among what imports, most are tiny scalar functions of musl's ctype/stdlib kind (abs, isascii, toascii) (70%).
Next, registered with its results: superopt (timed, defaults, 3 seeds × 60 s) on every function that imports and is measurable, and the census reasons say which front-end piece to add next.
Superopt part, registered after the census and before the run. The 6 non-stub functions that import (musl abs, isascii, toascii, is_leap, days_in_month; Lua filterpc) are copied verbatim to `tests/c_samples_musl_lua/` with their MIT notices (`LICENSES.md`). `is_leap` gets the `#include <limits.h>` its original file has at the top. `c_roundtrip.py` / `c_speedup.py` now drop `static` from the target before compiling it in its own unit (3 of the 6 are static). Round trip: 5/6 OK; days_in_month compares 511 inputs with no mismatch but trips the 50% guard: outside m in 1..32 its shift leaves [0, 31], undefined in C. External self-controls (inline): filterpc 1.004 (0.36 ns), abs 0.999 (0.29), days_in_month 1.001 (0.93), isascii 0.991 (0.33), is_leap 1.014 (0.74), toascii 0.903 (0.10 ns: unmeasurable, and already one `and`).
Run: `SAMPLES="tests/c_samples_musl_lua/*.c" scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_musl_lua_20261115 "10 11 12" 60 1 4`.
Predictions: all compiled-equivalent (95%); no result < 0.95 on the 5 measurable samples (90%: with no gain the original text is returned); gains (190 rule) on ≤ 1 of 5 (75%); is_leap gains (40%: `y % 100` / `y % 400` are the only real work, and the cheaper known forms, e.g. `% 25` and `& 15`, take several coordinated edits); abs, isascii, filterpc, days_in_month do not gain (80%: one to three instructions already).

### 192 (results): 0.6% of musl and 0.1% of Lua import on their own; the first real-code run returned wrong is_leap code

Census (`data/c_census_20261115/`): musl 12 / 2060 functions import alone (0.58%), Lua 1 / 1125 (0.09%). Reasons, musl: a type other than int 494 (pointer and library types: `void *`, `char *`, `FILE *`, `wchar_t`, `locale_t`, `pthread_*`), string or char literal 345 (`"` 254, `'` 91), integer literal suffix 200 (`1UL`, `0x...ULL`), `long` 197, return types and attributes the definition scanner does not know 170 (`ssize_t`, `wchar_t`, `_Noreturn`, `hidden`), `double` 79, no parameters 76, `char` 75, `size_t` 56, `float` 55; calls to functions outside the function 37. Lua: types 488 (`lua_State *` and friends), literals 354, unknown return types/attributes 228. What imports: 6 stubs (`return 0;`, `return fd;`), musl abs, isascii, toascii, is_leap (strftime.c), days_in_month (__tz.c), Lua filterpc (ldebug.c).
Predictions: musl < 3% — **held**. Lua < 2% — **held**. Top three reasons = non-int types, other pointers, external calls — **failed**: non-int types (pointers among them) first, then string/char literals and integer suffixes; external calls are only 37. What imports is tiny ctype/stdlib-like — **held**.
Superopt, first run (`data/superopt_musl_lua_20261115/`, pre-fix, kept as evidence): is_leap "gained" 1.81–2.63× inline on all 3 seeds, and all three are **wrong**. A native exhaustive comparison over all 2^32 arguments finds 4, 29 and 29 mismatches, first at 2147482100 / 2147481800. musl guards `if (y>INT_MAX-1900) y -= 2000;` against overflow; the candidates dropped the guard, and no test or CEGIS input (grid ±1024, specials, uniform samples) falls in that 1900-value tail. The battery's external check (`c_roundtrip.py`, 2000 random inputs) said EQUIVALENT too. This is the first wrong result returned by the superoptimizer; on real code, test-based equivalence was not enough. days_in_month: 1.46× (equivalent on its domain, checked with `--min-compared 0`), but `verified NO`, because `verifyGeneralization` counted a candidate error on an input the reference also fails on (the grid, then the +997 shift, leave m in 1..32) as a failure.
Fixes:
- CEGIS now also checks boundary inputs: the reference's constants with constant subexpressions folded (`INT_MAX - 1900` → 2147481747), c − 2 .. c + 2 in each argument, and the whole tail when a constant is within 4096 of an int limit.
- For a unary scalar function whose reference cannot fail at run time, a timed winner is accepted only after a compiled comparison on all 2^32 arguments (`CompiledTimer::exhaustive`, ~5 s); a refuted winner is skipped. `superopt` now prints `equivalence_level` (reference / exhaustive / tested) and `exhaustive_rejected`.
- `Problem::definesAnswer(input)` (default true, so every other problem is unchanged): in `evaluateInto`, an error or timeout on an input the problem defines no answer for is not a failure. ReferenceProblem returns whether the reference completes, and its scalar exhaustive grid keeps only in-domain inputs.
Checks: is_leap seeds 10 / 11 (60 s): 1.75× and 1.37×, both `exhaustive`. Seed 11's search had two timed winners refuted by the exhaustive check, each with 1 mismatch at −2147483600, far from any constant; the boundary inputs alone would not have caught them. The accepted seed-11 program replaces `y % 400 == 0` (given `y % 100 == 0`) by `((y + 12) & 12) == 0`, since 1900 ≡ 12 (mod 16): the known `& 15` leap-year trick, now proved on every argument. days_in_month seed 10: `verified yes`, 1.46×, level `tested` (two arguments). 308/308 tests.

### 192b (registered): the musl/Lua superopt battery rerun with the fixes

Run: `SAMPLES="tests/c_samples_musl_lua/*.c" scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_musl_lua2_20261115 "10 11 12" 60 1 4`. External equivalence for days_in_month is re-checked with `c_roundtrip.py --min-compared 0` (its domain is 1/3 of the random inputs); is_leap's outputs are also compared natively on all 2^32 arguments, outside superopt.
Predictions: every output equivalent, is_leap's on all 2^32 arguments (95%); is_leap gains on ≥ 2 seeds with level `exhaustive` (70%); days_in_month gains on ≥ 2 seeds (65%, it gained on all 3 before the fix); abs, isascii, filterpc do not gain (80%); no result < 0.95 on the 5 measurable samples (90%).

### 192b (results): is_leap 2.7–4.0× proved on all 2^32 arguments; earlier unary gains re-proved

Raw: `data/superopt_musl_lua2_20261115/`. (A retroactive check accidentally ran for a few seconds alongside the first search, lua_filterpc seed 10, and was stopped; that run returned the reference.)
18/18 outputs equivalent. days_in_month, checked with `--min-compared 0`, compares 506 in-domain inputs with no mismatch. is_leap's three outputs, compared natively on all 2^32 arguments outside superopt: 0 mismatches each.
Gains (190 rule, inline): is_leap 2.82 / 3.96 / 2.67, all three `exhaustive`; seed 10 had one timed winner refuted by the exhaustive check before a correct one was accepted. The accepted programs test `y & 3` first and reduce `y % 400` to `(y + 1900) & 15` (or `(y + 12) & 12`) under `y % 100 == 0`. days_in_month 3.11 / 3.07 on seeds 11 / 12, level `tested`. That one is not an algorithm: the program is the original's, and the compiled code differs only in the order of the two branch blocks (`jne` instead of `je`). The 3× appears when it is inlined into the timing loop, a codegen effect like sign's in 189. abs, isascii, filterpc, toascii: reference returned, 0.99–1.00.
Predictions: all equivalent, is_leap on all 2^32 — **held**. is_leap gains on ≥ 2 seeds, `exhaustive` — **held** (3). days_in_month gains on ≥ 2 seeds — **held** (2, codegen, not algorithmic). abs, isascii, filterpc no gain — **held**. No result < 0.95 — **held** (lowest 0.992).
Retroactive check: every unary-function output that `superopt` returned as a gain in 190b (ON and OFF) and 191 was compared natively on all 2^32 arguments. That is 25 programs (sign, reverse_bits8, is_pow2_loop, count_trailing_zeros, popcount_loop, count_digits): 0 mismatches in every one. The is_leap failure was specific to an input region the tests never reach; the earlier claims stand.
Reading: on the first real-code function where the search found a real algorithmic rewrite (is_leap, a known trick), test-based equivalence returned wrong code. Exhaustive compiled checking caught it, and so did, one case in three, the boundary inputs. For unary functions, the level `exhaustive` makes the result a proof. Functions of two or more arguments stay `tested` (phase 5, SMT/BMC). The census says the front end reaches very little real code: under 1% of musl and Lua functions import on their own. The largest gates are types (pointers, `unsigned`, `char`, `long`), string and char literals, and integer suffixes.

### 193 (registered): character literals in the front end

The lexer now reads a character literal as an int constant: one ASCII character or escape (`\n \t \r \0 \a \b \f \v \\ \' \" \?`, octal `\ooo`, hex `\xHH`). Values above 127 are rejected (their int value depends on whether `char` is signed), and so are multi-character, empty and unknown-escape literals. Test `c_import_character_literals_are_int_constants`; 309/309 tests.
Census prediction, registered before the rerun: `'` was the first gate for 91 musl and 79 Lua functions (192), and most of them also use `char`, `unsigned` or pointers, so +2 to +8 musl functions import on their own and +0 to +2 Lua (70%).
Superopt part, registered after the census and before the run: what newly imports (non-stub) goes verbatim to `tests/c_samples_musl_lua/`. Run: `SAMPLES="tests/c_samples_musl_lua/{musl_isblank,lua_digit}.c"`-style battery, 3 seeds × 60 s, `--time-top 4`, as in 192b. Predictions: both outputs equivalent and `exhaustive` or the reference (95%); neither gains (75%: both bodies are already branch-free and gcc compiles `'0' <= c && c <= '9'` to one unsigned compare).

### 193 (results): char literals open 2 functions; isblank 1.20× proved, but from codegen

Census (`data/c_census_char_20261115/`, same sources and directories as 192; the census prediction was stated before the rerun and written to the log right after it): musl 13 / 2060 (+1: `isblank`), Lua 2 / 1125 (+1: `digit`, lstrlib.c). `'` stops being the first gate for 82 musl and 39 Lua functions, which then stop at the next one: a type other than int (23 / 20), an integer suffix (17 / 2), `char` (16 / 4), an unknown definition head (8 / 9), `unsigned` as a cast or a type (10 / 0). The `"` (string) gate is unchanged, 263 musl.
Prediction +2 to +8 musl — **failed** (+1). +0 to +2 Lua — **held** (+1).
Superopt (`data/superopt_char_20261115/`, `SAMPLES="tests/c_samples_musl_lua/musl_isblank.c tests/c_samples_musl_lua/lua_digit.c" scripts/run_superopt_battery.sh data/superopt_char_20261115 "10 11 12" 60 1 4`): 6/6 equivalent. lua_digit: reference returned on all 3 seeds (inline 1.00, self 1.02). musl_isblank: inline 1.208 / 1.200 / 1.205, level `exhaustive` (self inline 1.006, ref 0.81 ns; call mode, 0.01–0.04 ns, is unmeasurable). All three programs are `(c == 32) + (c == 9)`: `||` replaced by `+` on two disjoint comparisons. The compiled code has the same two `cmp`/`sete`; the original joins them with `or` and zero-extends the byte (`movzbl`) at the end, the candidate zeroes both registers with `xor` first and adds. A register-level codegen difference, like days_in_month's in 192b, not an algorithm.
Predictions: both equivalent, `exhaustive` or the reference — **held**. Neither gains — **failed** (isblank, by the 190 rule, from codegen).
Reading: char literals by themselves open almost nothing; they are needed, but the functions that use them almost always also use `char`, `unsigned` or pointers. The next gate to remove is `unsigned` (casts like `(unsigned)c - 'a' < 26` are the musl ctype idiom), then integer suffixes.

### 194 (registered): 32-bit `unsigned` in the front end

The translator types every expression as int or unsigned (`unsigned`, `unsigned int`, `uint32_t`, casts between them), with C's usual arithmetic conversions. Mini-C values keep the int with the same bits. Only these operators change:
- ordered comparisons, where either side is unsigned, lower to `(a ^ INT_MIN) < (b ^ INT_MIN)`;
- `>>` of an unsigned left operand is logical: `(a >> k) & (0xFFFFFFFF >> k)` for a constant count, `(a >> n) & ~((INT_MIN >> n) << 1)` for a variable one (still an error outside 0..31);
- unsigned `/` and `%` are rejected for now.

Literals follow C for 32-bit int: a `u`/`U` suffix gives unsigned, an unsuffixed hex or octal literal above INT_MAX is unsigned, and a decimal one is still rejected (it would be long). `L` suffixes and `unsigned char/short/long` are still rejected. Helpers have typed parameters and results. The emitter writes the original signature (`unsigned f(unsigned x)`), with int bits inside (gcc converts modulo 2^32). `prefixFunctions` and the scripts' `linkable()` recognize the new return types. Test `c_import_unsigned_compares_shifts_literals_and_helpers` (9 functions × 676 argument pairs, against native unsigned C++); 310/310.
